# Changelog

## Unreleased — Performance, stage 1: the bar's window is the strip, not the whole screen

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Measured on the real shell before the change (`tests/perf-test/gpu-attribution.py`, `bisect-gpu.sh`, 1080p / 144 Hz, Hyprland on the AMD iGPU, nothing playing): Hyprland 23 % of the GPU with everything on, 21 % with the dock hidden, 6 % with the dock and bar hidden, 3 % with only the bar hidden; `qs` itself under 1 % in all of them. Stopping the shell took Hyprland to 0.6 %. So the idle cost was Hyprland compositing the bar's surface, which was as big as the screen (`implicitHeight = screen.height`).

- Changed: the bar window is now the strip plus 24 px of room (for the popups' anchor points), anchored to its edge as before, so the strip is in the same place and every position in `Bar.qml` (mask, `popupX` / `popupY`, the status bar's x / y) is unchanged. It is the whole screen again while this monitor is in Edit Desktop, because `EditStage` shrinks the picture into the card from the window's size, and goes back after Done (when `EditMode.screenName` clears).
- New setting `bar.compactWindow` (default on). **If anything is off: `qs -c pjsekai ipc call options set bar.compactWindow false`** restores the screen-sized window at once.
- Check first: the tray dropdown and the tray right-click menu on a top bar (their anchor point is further below the strip than the other popups; if one goes missing or lands wrong, that is the cause), the other bar popups, Edit Desktop in and out (look for a jump on the bar when Done is pressed), a preset swap sliding the bar out and in, Game Mode, a vertical bar if you use one.
- Not changed: the dock (still 96 % of the screen high; hiding it did not change the idle load), the widgets, the wallpaper.
- Files: `modules/sekai/bar/Bar.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `tests/perf-test/gpu-attribution.py`, `tests/perf-test/bisect-gpu.sh`.

## Unreleased — Merge: the welcome logo's stroke fix on top of pjsekai_white_line_7

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Three-way merge with 2x2_3 as the starting point. In `white_line_7` the only files of the logo's that differed from my last version were `WelcomeLogo.qml` and its test, and every line that was yours-only in them was the old blur-cut stroke the fix replaces: nothing of yours was edited there, so there was nothing to reconcile. Taken: `WelcomeLogo.qml`, `shaders/welcome_halo.frag` (+ `.qsb`), `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js`. Everything else in `white_line_7` (the white line's one thickness, the bar, the presets, the media controls, the Weather and Calendar changes and the rest) is untouched. Every node test that passed before still passes (the welcome test has the stroke's new checks); `tests/analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed before the merge, and still does. **Run `./shaders/compile-shaders.sh`** (the new shader).

## Unreleased — Welcome logo: the light stroke is an even outline that follows the letters, its halftone wraps round them, and nothing is cut off

From a screenshot of the real shell: the stroke's halftone ended in a hard vertical edge on the right (and the stroke behind the kana in a flat edge on top), and the stroke was a wobbly blob (thick round "Pop", thin round "Sekai") instead of a band wrapped round the letters. I could not reproduce your exact screenshot (no Google Sans Flex here, Poppins stands in), so the cause is read from where the edge sat: about 70 px right of the last letter, which is where the logo's drawing area ended. Checked as before: rendered frame by frame with Qt 6.12 and the compiled shaders, not run on a live Quickshell; `welcome_unit.js` passes.

- Fixed: **the stroke was cut off at the edge of the logo's drawing area.** Everything of the logo is drawn into a box with a margin of 56 px round the word; the stroke and its shadow reached past it, and what reaches past is cut flat. The margin is 80 px now (`pad`), checked by a test against the stroke's width, its shadow and the kana, which stand highest.
- Changed: **the stroke is a true outline, not a blur cut at a level.** The silhouette of the whole logo (letters, rims, kana, sparkles) is drawn 28 times round a circle and the union is the stroke: the same width all the way round every curve, no blob, no bridging between letters. It grows from the letters outward as it comes in, and in again on the way out, so it hugs them the whole time (it used to scale the whole shape, which slid it off the letters). Width 17 px at 1080p (`strokeFull`). Its mint shadow, down and to the right, and its halftone are cut to the same outline.
- Changed: **the stroke's halftone wraps round the letters** (new `shaders/welcome_halo.frag`, replaces `welcome_dots.frag` for the stroke): the dots are biggest right beside the letters, shrink to nothing before the stroke's outer edge (so no dot is ever cut through) and swell on the lower right of every letter, like a shadow. The matte band that crosses it while it comes up still swells the dots under it, fading toward the edge. Before, it was one lattice shaded from the bottom right corner of the whole box, which is what ended in the hard edge.
- Fixed: a glint in the middle of its run when the exit started stopped where it was and hung over the letters while they collapsed; it is now reset when the idling stops.
- Without `welcome_halo.frag.qsb` the stroke is still the even outline, just without its dots. **Run `./shaders/compile-shaders.sh`** (a new shader).
- Files: `WelcomeLogo.qml`, `shaders/welcome_halo.frag` (+ `.qsb`), `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js`.

## Unreleased — One thickness for the white line, taken from the clock

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** All the node tests still pass (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). **The clock is unchanged** (its digit blocks and its sticker numerals are drawn exactly as before). **Nothing was drawn**, so look at: the weather and calendar plates, the GOOD AFTERNOON chip, the weather card's "30" and "°C", "October" on the calendar, and the user card's UPTIME / SHELL / DESKTOP blocks, the weather disc and the calendar's keys. They should show the same white line as the clock's digit blocks.

- Why: the white line differed from item to item. The clock's digit blocks had 2 px, the other blocks 1 px, and sticker text scaled it with its size (the 64 px "30" about 3 px, the smaller texts 1.5 px).
- Now there is **one number**, `Theme.whiteLine` (2 px, the clock's digit blocks' line), in `modules/common/Theme.qml`. `StickerText` uses it at every size, and the other blocks write their ring as `Theme.borderWidth + Theme.whiteLine` (they were 1 px, now 2 px). The clock's digit blocks are written the same way and stay at 2 px. Change that one number to change them all together.
- Also the **big pink plates** on the weather and calendar cards (`SekaiPlate`'s default ring was 1 px, now `Theme.borderWidth + Theme.whiteLine`; the bar popups' plates follow because they share it) and the user card's **GOOD AFTERNOON chip** (it had no white line in this build, now it has the same one).
- Not changed: the clock (the sticker numerals keep their own 3.2 px line), the navy outline widths, the wide pale ring around each card, Dark mode's inner ring (`Theme.cardRing`), and the blocks that have no white line (ringWidth 3). `StickerText` is shared, so the popups, the lock screen and the wallpaper wheel get the same white line too.

## Unreleased — Calendar card 2 x 2: double-clicking a day no longer changes the card's size

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** All the node tests still pass (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). Check: double-click a day on the 2 x 2 calendar; the card should stay 2 x 2.

- Fixed: a double-click on a day of the 2 x 2 calendar switched the card to its full size (to open the add form there), and that size was then saved. The size now only changes when you change it (Settings -> Desktop, the size key in Edit Desktop, or ipc). Single clicks, the wheel and the month name work as before.
- Removed: `addFromMini()` in `CalendarCardWidget.qml` and the mini face's `onDoubleClicked`. To add an entry, put the card on its full size first.

## Unreleased — One rule for old presets: a new option never lands on a preset saved before it existed

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** All the node tests pass (`preset-widgets-test`, `preset-motion-test`, `weather-card-test`, `preset-frames-test` and the rest, except `analyzer-test/chart_test.js`, which already failed). The new cases fail again when the rule is switched off in `PresetParts.js`. **Nothing was drawn**, so the things to check are: wear a preset saved before this build while the weather card is on Minimal (it should shrink away and come back as Classic, with the tick on that preset), and the weather card's new **Classic** label in Settings -> Desktop and on the card's style key.

- Why: there were two rules. Most options left an old preset alone (it kept whatever the widget was set to now), and a few had their own hand-written exception (the weather look went back to Full, the visualizer's direction to a band along the bottom, the to-do's size kept the current one). Which one you got depended on the widget.
- Now there is **one rule**: a preset saved before an option existed wears the way things were back then, whatever the widget is set to now. Each widget lists that in one line, `before`, in its entry in `services/PresetWidgets.qml`:
  - weather card `before: { style: "classic" }`
  - to-do, calendar card, palette card `before: { size: "full" }`
  - visualizer `before: { vertical: false, bandX: -1 }` (a band along the bottom, as before)
- It is used everywhere the preset is read: wearing it, the "you are wearing this one" tick, and the shrink / pop animation (`DesktopPresetService.motionPlan` no longer has a weather special case; it reads the same `before`). Added to `PresetParts.js`: `fillBefore`, `readPart`; removed: `withDirection` and the three hand-written `normalize` lines (`normalize` is now only for a value that was renamed).
- **To add a new option to a widget:** add it to that widget's `before` with what it was before. That is the only step. The startup check (`PP.problems`) reports a `before` that names something the widget doesn't save, so a typo can't silently do nothing.
- **Changed behaviour:** wearing a preset saved before the to-do, calendar card or palette card had a size now puts that card on `full` (it used to leave the size where it was). A preset saved before the visualizer could stand up now makes a standing band lie down along the bottom, with the swap animation (the animation used to skip it).
- **The weather card's "Full" is now called "Classic"** (Settings -> Desktop -> Weather card style, the card's STYLE key, `ipc call weathercard style classic`). Saves and presets that say `full` still work and read as classic; `ipc call weathercard style full` also still works.
- Not changed: every other widget option; a save file from before this build keeps whatever the card has; what a preset holds; the clock's design and the visualizer's own style / size are not part of this (they were never added later).
- Files: `modules/common/functions/PresetParts.js`, `services/PresetWidgets.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `services/WeatherCardService.qml`, `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/WeatherCardWidget.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `modules/sekai/editMode/SizeKey.qml`, `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatBinds.js`, `shell.qml`; tests in `tests/preset-widgets-test`, `preset-motion-test`, `weather-card-test`, `preset-frames-test`, `tests/lib/presetparts.js`.

## Unreleased — Weather card: a Minimal look (same 2 x 2 size)

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** `tests/weather-card-test/style_unit.js` (new, 34 checks) passes, `preset-motion-test` got new cases for the look (old presets included) and `preset-widgets-test/registry_unit.js` got a check that an old preset wears Full and ticks as Full (both fail if the fix is taken out), and every other node test gives the same result as before (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). I broke the new test on purpose two ways (the plate taller so the tab runs into the lane, an id used in both looks) and it caught both; the Full look's code was compared with the old file and is identical apart from its indentation. **Nothing was drawn**, so the first things to check are: the Minimal card at 80 / 100 / 150 % scale, a 3-character temperature (`-12°C`, `100°F`) against the disc, a long place name on the tab, and the card with no connection (the lane says what it is waiting for).

- Added: **a Minimal look for the weather card.** The same housing (navy outline, pale ring, hard shadow), the same gradient plate, tilted disc (poke it: it squashes and throws diamonds), slanted place tab and refresh key, with far less on it: the temperature as a big sticker numeral with its unit, the conditions above it, today's high and low under the disc, and **one lane with the next four readings** (time, glyph, temperature; the first is "now" on a mint tab). No humidity tank, wind dial, UV meter, Hourly / Days switch or hover readout. Still the 286 x 238 content, so it is exactly the same 2 x 2 footprint as before and swaps in place.
- Switch it: **Settings -> Desktop -> Weather card look**, the **look key** on the card's top-right corner while it is selected in Edit Desktop (the clock's design key, made reusable: it shows the look it will switch to and a chip names the look you ended on), or `qs -c pjsekai ipc call weathercard style minimal` (or `full`).
- Remembered across restarts (`weathercard.json` gets a `style` key; a file without it stays Full) and **kept by desktop presets**: a preset saved with Minimal wears Minimal, and a preset that changes the look makes the card shrink away and pop back in, like the clock's design.
- **Presets saved before the Minimal style wear classic** (the style was called Full at first; see the section above for the rule that does this).
- Only the look in use is built (two Loaders), so the Minimal card is also the lighter one: no curve canvas, no gauge tiles, no day rows.
- If wttr.in sends a reading but no forecast, the lane shows humidity and wind as plain figures instead of leaving a hole.
- Not changed: the Full look (every line of it is the same), where the card sits, its drag / lock / Edit Desktop behaviour, Focus Mode, the weather source and its settings, the bar's weather pill and popup.
- Files: `services/WeatherCardService.qml` (`style`, `minimal`, `setStyle`, `toggleStyle`, saved and restored), `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/WeatherCardWidget.qml` (the two looks as Components behind Loaders, the look key), `modules/sekai/editMode/SizeKey.qml` (optional `icon` / `label`; the size users are untouched), `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `shell.qml` (ipc `style`), `services/PresetWidgets.qml` (the `weathercard` entry's `normalize`), `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (motion plan), `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatBinds.js`, `tests/weather-card-test/style_unit.js` (new), `tests/preset-motion-test/preset_motion_unit.js`, `tests/preset-widgets-test/registry_unit.js` and `tests/preset-frames-test/frames_unit.js` (their fake weather card now holds a `style`, as the real one does). Undo: put those files back from `pjsekai_widget_registry.zip` and delete the new test.
- Tune: the plate's height `plateH`, the lane's top `laneY` and the number of readings `cols` at the top of the minimal face in `WeatherCardWidget.qml` (the lane runs to the bottom of the card, so moving `laneY` resizes it); the numeral's `pixelSize` (64) and the disc's size (72 x 74) in the same place.

## Unreleased — One list of desktop widgets for presets: a new widget needs one entry, and old presets switch it off

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** `tests/preset-widgets-test/registry_unit.js` (new) passes, and so do the other preset tests (`preset-frames-test` was updated to run the real widget list); every other node test gives the same result as before (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). I broke the code on purpose five ways (a widget with no entry, a shell widget nobody registered, the "switch it off" rule removed, a service without `setShown`, the tick fill removed) and the tests caught each one. **Nothing was drawn**, so the first things to check are: wear a preset saved before the Calendar card / Palette card / Sticky Notes / Image frames existed while they are showing (they should switch off), then wear one saved today (they should come back), and look at `qs log -c pjsekai` for a line starting `DesktopPresetService:` at start (it means a widget entry is half-made).

- The rule was already there for four widgets (a preset saved before a widget existed has no part for it, and wearing it switches the widget OFF), but it was spread over seven places in `DesktopPresetService.qml`, so the next widget would have needed edits in all of them and forgetting one left it on. Now it is **one rule in one place** (`modules/common/functions/PresetParts.js`) and **one list** (`services/PresetWidgets.qml`): saving, wearing, the "you are wearing this one" tick and old presets all follow from a widget's entry. `DesktopPresetService.qml` lost `widgetSkip`, `laterWidgets`, `laterWidgetNow`, `widgetPart`, `restoreWidget` and `withDirection` (all moved).
- **Adding a widget is now:** give its service `shown` / `setShown()` / `snapshot()` / `restore()` / `persist()`, add one line to `PresetWidgets.qml` (`{ id: "mywidget", service: MyWidgetService, skip: [...] }`), run the test. The how-to is at the top of `PresetWidgets.qml`.
- New: **a widget nobody registered is caught.** The test reads `shell.qml` (every `widgetId`) and Edit Desktop's widget list and fails when one has no entry, and when an entry's service lacks `shown` / `setShown()` (or `snapshot` / `restore` / `persist` where it needs them). At start the shell also prints a line for a half-made entry.
- Fixed: **a newer option inside an existing widget no longer takes the tick away from older presets.** A preset saved before the clock (say) gained an option never held it, so it could never show "this is the one you're wearing" again. A key the preset can't hold now counts as the widget's own value, the same value wearing the preset leaves it at.
- Fixed: **one widget that fails can't stop a save or leave the rest of a preset unworn.** Each widget is saved and worn on its own `try`; a failure is logged and that widget is skipped.
- Changed (rare): the five widgets that were there from the start (clock, user card, weather, to-do, visualizer) follow the same rule now: a preset that has a `widgets` part but none for one of them (a hand-edited or damaged file) switches it off. Before, it was left alone. Presets the shell saves always hold all of them.
- Not changed: what a preset holds, its file format (`_meta.v` is still 1), presets already saved, the tick for the bar / dock / phone / overview / pictures, the motion of wearing a preset (`PresetMotion` still has its own flag per widget; a new widget does not need one and uses its normal show / hide animation).
- Files: `services/PresetWidgets.qml` (new), `modules/common/functions/PresetParts.js` (new), `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `tests/preset-widgets-test/registry_unit.js` (new), `tests/lib/presetparts.js` and `tests/lib/registry.js` (new), `tests/preset-frames-test/frames_unit.js`. Undo: put those files back from `pjsekai_presets_overview.zip` and delete the new ones.

## Unreleased — Presets keep the to-do's size, every Custom Image frame, the phone's style and settings, and the Overview

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** `tests/preset-frames-test/frames_unit.js` (new, covers the phone part too) passes, `preset-state.sh` was run for real in a scratch folder (save, rename, restore, delete), and every other node test gives the same result as before (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). **Nothing was drawn**, so the first things to check are wearing two presets one after the other with different image frames, one saved in 2D next to one saved in 2.5D, and one saved with the cork board next to one saved with Scroll.

- Fixed: **a preset now keeps the to-do's size** (full / 2x2). It held the card's spot but not its size, so wearing a preset left the card at whatever size it had. The calendar card and the visualizer already kept their sizes.
- Fixed: **a preset now keeps its own Custom Image frames**. It used to hold only "images shown: on / off", so every preset showed the same frames. Each preset now holds every frame (spot, size, crop, rounding, border, shadow, opacity, which monitor) and wearing it swaps your frames for that preset's. Empty frames are kept too.
- How the pictures are kept: the shell deletes a picture's file when its frame is removed, so a preset keeps its own copies in `<name>.frames/` beside `<name>.json` (same idea as the avatar and banner in `<name>.images/`). Wearing a preset puts them back in the data folder; the old frames' files that the new ones don't use are deleted (the preset you came from still has its copies). Rename moves the folder, delete removes it.
- **Presets saved before this** hold no frames and no to-do size: wearing one leaves your image frames and the to-do's size alone (the on / off switch still applies), and its tick still shows. **Save them again** to keep the frames and size.
- A frame whose picture copy is gone is left out when wearing; an empty frame needs none.
- Fixed: **a preset now keeps the Quick Panel phone's style and settings**: 2.5D or flat 2D (Settings -> Quick -> Phone), hot edge on / off, edge delay, and type to search apps. They were on the list of things a preset never touches, so every preset wore whatever phone you had. They are kept as their own `phone` part of the preset (the four keys stay in `keepExact`, so an older preset can't reset them). How much of the phone shows and the notification style were already part of a preset. A preset saved before this has no `phone` part and leaves your phone alone; save it again to keep it.
- Fixed: **a preset now keeps the Overview's style and options**: cork board or Scroll (niri) as the style, the board's side of the screen, how many workspace cards it shows, and the Scroll plugin's layout / scale / gap. They were held back by the `overview.` rule, so every preset wore whatever Overview you had. Same arrangement as the phone: their own `overview` part (the `overview.` prefix stays in `keepPrefix`, so an older preset can't reset them); a preset saved before this leaves your Overview alone, save it again to keep it. Wearing one puts the Scroll plugin's options on the plugin at once (`ScrollOverviewService.applyConfig()`); an option that was "not set" in the preset stays "not set" (the plugin's own config is left alone, so it keeps its live value until the next config reload).
- Not changed: the sticky notes (on / off only, their content is yours), the to-do's tasks, the avatar / banner picker (`files.chooser`, a machine setting).
- Files: `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (`phonePart` / `wearPhone`, `overviewPart` / `wearOverview`), `services/ImageService.qml` (new `wearFrames`), `scripts/state/preset-state.sh` (frames in `save`, new `restore-frames`, `rename` and `delete`), `tests/preset-frames-test/frames_unit.js` (new), `tests/preset-images-test/preset_images_unit.js`, `tests/preset-favorite-test/favorite_unit.js`. Undo: put those files back from `pjsekai_image_drop.zip`.

## Unreleased — Custom Image: an empty frame on the desktop at once, drop files onto it

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Brackets balanced in every edited file; `tests/image-test/image_unit.js` passes (new checks for empty frames, for sorting what a drop carries, and for the wiring read from source), and every other node test gives the same result as before (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). **Nothing was drawn and nothing was dragged**, so the first things to check are: that a file dragged from your file manager is accepted by the frame (the Wayland drag-and-drop path is the part that could not be tried here), the dashed outline's corners, and BROWSE's place on a small frame.

- Changed: **the shelf's Image tile no longer opens a file chooser first.** It puts an empty frame on the desktop right away (320 x 240, less on a small screen), on the monitor being edited, selected. Same for Settings -> Desktop -> Widgets (now "Add an image frame") and the new `qs -c pjsekai ipc call image add`.
- Added: **drop picture files on a frame.** The empty frame is a card with a dashed outline and "DROP A PICTURE HERE"; it lights up mint ("RELEASE TO ADD") while a file is held over it. The first picture goes into the frame, which keeps its spot and width and takes the picture's ratio. Dropping on a frame that already has a picture replaces it ("DROP TO REPLACE", the same as the panel's swap key; the old copy is deleted, your original file never is). Several files in one drop: the first fills the frame, each of the others becomes a new frame, stepping down and right.
- Added: **a drop that is not a picture says so** on the frame: "NOT A PICTURE", or "n FILES SKIPPED" when some of a drop were pictures and some were not (a folder, a text file, a web address).
- Kept: the file chooser. **BROWSE** on the empty frame (and the swap key in the panel) opens it for that frame; `ipc call image pick` and `image addPath <file>` work as before.
- Persistence: an empty frame is remembered like any other (spot, size, screen) and is still there after a restart. `ImageMath.normalize` keeps a record whose path is `""` (a record with no `path` at all is still dropped).
- Imports are now a queue: a drop of ten pictures is copied one after the other and none is lost (`addPath` goes through it too, it used to be ignored while another copy was running).
- How: `ImageService.addEmpty()`, `drop()`, `reject()` and the import queue; `ImageWidget.qml` gets a `DropArea`, the empty face, BROWSE (above the drag area, under Edit Desktop's shield) and the reject note; `ImageMath.js` gets `emptyFrame`, `localPath`, `isPicture`, `sortDrop`. The copy itself is still `scripts/images/pick-custom-image.sh import`, unchanged (GIFs are not re-encoded).
- Not changed: how a picture is shown, cropped, rounded, bordered or saved; presets (they only remember whether images are shown).
- Files: `services/ImageService.qml`, `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/ImageWidget.qml`, `modules/common/functions/ImageMath.js`, `services/EditMode.qml`, `shell.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatBinds.js`, `tests/image-test/image_unit.js`. Undo: put those files back from `pjsekai_capsule_vertical.zip`.
- Tune: the first size `EMPTY_W` / `EMPTY_H` in `ImageMath.js`; the dashed outline's inset and dash pattern at the top of `emptyFace` in `ImageWidget.qml`.

## Unreleased — Capsule bar style on a standing bar (left / right edge)

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Brackets balanced in every edited file; `tests/capsule-test/capsule_source_test.js` (now also checks the standing bar's numbers and wiring) and `capsule_popup_test.js` pass, and every other node test that passed before still passes (`analyzer-test/chart_test.js` already failed). **Nothing was drawn**, so the standing bar's look is the first thing to check: the width of the chips against their text, the ends of the rounded bar, and the dots column.

- Changed: **Capsule is no longer drawn as Float on a left / right bar.** It is the same slim, fully rounded bar standing up: 3 px rim, the body, a hard shadow 3 px below it, 8 px off the screen edge, 16 px in from the screen's ends. Colours are the same theme tokens (rim = body x 1.87, shadow = body x 1.42).
- Sizes (design px, `Theme.cap*V*`): chips are **40** wide (`capVChip`), 5 px between a chip and the rim (`capVPadX`), the first / last chip 8 px from the rim along the bar (`capVPadY`), so the bar's face is **56** wide and its strip **72** (8 + 56 + 8, `capStripV`). Gaps between chips are the same 6 as on a flat bar.
- Workspaces: the ringed dots become a **column**; the active one is a tall pill (34 px) instead of a wide one. With many workspaces the column still shows only the dots around the active one, as many as fit.
- Every other module is a flat, fully rounded chip, as on the flat bar, but **the whole box tall** (not 21) so the two-line texts fit: clock, date, battery, weather, CPU / RAM, status, privacy, Discord, the pinned tray, the Utility pill and the active-window key; the media chip is a round chip with the media glyph; the notification and updates badges are round.
- Popups: beside a standing Capsule bar they sit 8 px from the bar (the strip already holds the gap); their cards wear the bar's colours as before.
- How: `Status_Bar.qml`'s `capsule` is now true on any edge and it adds `capsuleVertical`; `Pill.qml` gets `capsuleV` (the chip is the box less its shadow room, rounded by the shorter side) and `barVWidth` (40 in Capsule, 52 otherwise); `Bar.qml` no longer maps Capsule to Float on a standing bar. `Theme.capsuleVerticalFor(item)` mirrors `capsuleFor`.
- Not changed: the flat Capsule bar (all its numbers are the same), the other three styles, the dock's and the notification popups' distance from a bar (they use the edge's usual thickness, as they did for the flat Capsule bar).

  - **Keys**: previous, a wide Play / Pause in the song's colours, next, as keycaps that press into their shadow; a key the player cannot do is dimmed. Two small tool keys top right: the equalizer and the player's own window (workspace and all).

Nothing new runs while the card is closed.

## Unreleased — Capsule bar style (slim rounded bar, dot workspaces, flat chip pills)

**Merged onto `pjsekai_2x2_3_merged`** (none of the files this touches had changed there, so it applied without conflicts). **Not run on a live Quickshell.** Brackets balanced in every edited file; the new `tests/capsule-test/capsule_source_test.js` checks the wiring and the measured numbers, and the wheel / palette source tests still pass. **Nothing was drawn by Quickshell**, so the look on your screen is the first thing to check. The geometry was measured off the reference screenshot and redrawn separately at the same size; the bar, dots and clock chip lined up to within a pixel.

- New: **a fourth bar style, Capsule** (Settings -> Bar -> Style, Settings -> Quick, or `qs -c pjsekai ipc call barstyle set capsule`). A slim, fully rounded bar: 36 px tall (3 rim + 30 body + 3 rim) over a 3 px hard shadow, 8 px off the screen edge, inset by the same 16 px as Float.
- New: **the pills change with it.** Workspaces are ringed dots (12 px of colour in a 3 px ring, 6 apart); the active one is a wide pill (28 px of colour) that glides open. Every other module is a flat, fully rounded chip 21 tall with no outline, ring or shadow; their icons are drawn at most 16 px. Workspace colours are fixed pastels (`Theme.capDotColors`), one per workspace.
- The colours are not hard-coded: body = the theme's own surface, rim = body x 1.87, shadow = body x 1.42 (the screenshot's `#2e233f` / `#564078` / `#42315c` are exactly those ratios), so every group theme and matugen gets its own tint. On a plum theme it lands close to the screenshot; to pin the screenshot's exact hex values, set `capBody` / `capRim` / `capShade` in `Theme.qml`.
- The bar reserves a slimmer strip (8 + 39 + 8 = 55 px, `Theme.capStrip`), minus Hyprland's `gaps_out`, so windows sit about 8 px under the bar's shadow.
- **Flat bars only.** On a bar standing on the left or right edge, Capsule draws as Float.
- **Simplified in this style:** the Active Window chip shows the app name on one line (no title line); the media chip shows a music glyph and the title instead of cover art and the artist line; scroll hints are off. Chips that mean something keep their colour (Discord call, mic / screen in use, updates waiting).
- How it works: `Status_Bar.qml` sets `capsuleBar` while the style is up, and `Pill.qml`, `MaterialIcon.qml` and `WorkspaceBadges.qml` ask `Theme.capsuleFor(item)` (the way `classicFor` finds `classicPanel`) and redraw themselves. Popups are not under the bar, so they are unchanged.

## Unreleased — Welcome logo, second pass: one set of textures for the whole word, outlines behind all faces, one wave of floating

Same way of checking as the first pass (the entry "Welcome logo: rebuilt as separately animated layers", further down): rendered frame by frame with a real Qt 6.12 and the compiled shaders (Poppins standing in for the shell's font), `welcome_unit.js` passes; not run on a live Quickshell, speed not measured. Run `./shaders/compile-shaders.sh` again (`welcome_dots.frag` changed).

What was wrong: every letter ran its own halftone, wave, shine and extrusion on its own pop timeline, so while some letters were still flying in, the ones that had landed were already at a different stage, the dots of neighbouring letters did not line up, the shine lit one letter at a time, and each letter's outline fell over its neighbours' faces.

- Changed: **the textures belong to the word, not to the letters.** One wave texture (one phase, one wavelength in the logo's coordinates, so the ripple runs through every letter and both lines), one halftone (one dot lattice over the word's rectangle, so the dots line up from letter to letter, the shading is in the word's lower right and ONE matte band crosses the whole word) and one hard shine (two slanted bands crossing both lines left to right), all on one clock (`FX_*` in `WelcomeMath.js`: wave 1.0 s, shading 1.1 s, matte 1.25 s, shine 1.5 s). They are shown only through the letters' faces (a track matte), so a letter that lands late is already inside the running texture. The idle glint is the same matte and shine again, as one pass across the word.
- Changed: **every letter is built as two halves that move exactly alike**: a back (gradient rim and extrusion) and a face (fill and gloss), `part: "back"` / `"face"` in `WelcomeLetter.qml`. All the backs of a line sit behind all its faces, so no letter's outline falls over a neighbour any more; the second line is still in front of the first. `part: "all"` (the default, used by the icon on the second slide) is the old whole letter with its own textures.
- Changed: **the letters no longer float each on a phase of its own**: one slow wave of floating runs through the word (`idleSkew`), each letter a little later than the one to its left, so the word moves as one piece.
- Changed: the combined wave and sweep pass moved to 2.0 s / 2.15 s so it does not run into the textures; the dot shader has a `falloff` (how far the shading reaches from the corner; 0.78 for a letter, 1.2 for the word).
- Learned (kept as comments where it matters): a `ShaderEffectSource` whose `sourceItem` is set to `null` stops hiding the item it was hiding, so an unused part is emptied, not unhooked; and effects nested inside an item hidden by `hideSource` do not draw, so the faces are drawn directly and their texture copy is only the matte.
- Files: `WelcomeLogo.qml`, `WelcomeLetter.qml`, `WelcomePopLetter.qml`, `WelcomeMath.js`, `shaders/welcome_dots.frag` (+ `.qsb`), `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js`.

## Unreleased — One scroll feel for the whole shell

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Brackets balanced in every edited file (a `[`/`]` count that looked off in `ReloadPopup.qml` is the same in the zip you sent); every node test that passed before still passes, and the new `tests/wheel-test/wheel_source_test.js` checks the wiring. **Nothing was scrolled for real**, so the feel itself is the first thing to check.

- Fixed: **scrolling sensitivity differed from one place to the next.** The shell already had one shared wheel layer (`WheelScroll`, a mouse notch = `Theme.wheelStep` = 110 px, a touchpad 1:1), but only some lists used it. The rest were on Qt's own wheel, which moves less per notch, so those felt like they needed a lot of scrolling: the **app drawer** (`LauncherFace`), the **emoji picker** (`EmojiCard`), the **wallpaper gallery** grid (`WallpaperFace`), the **Calendar card's event list**, the **To-do list** and the **reload popup's error box**. Each now has `WheelScroll`.
- Fixed: **a second set of areas used their own maths** (a flat 120 px a notch, and a touchpad's real finger distance ignored): the **Sticky Note** text, the **Cheat Sheet**, and the sideways rows in **Settings** (chip strip), **Edit Desktop** (theme and preset boards), the **Palette** tool and **Palette card** favourites. They now take their distance from one function, `Theme.wheelPx(event, sideways)`. Overview and the Edit Desktop shelf already matched and now call it too (no change in feel).
- `WheelScroll` also learned `orientation: Qt.Horizontal`, and now hides / disables itself when its list does (before, a faded-out or switched-off list under it kept taking the wheel).
- **Left alone on purpose:** wheel actions that are not scrolling (workspace switch, month paging in the bar's Calendar popup, media / visualizer volume, tray icon scroll, the Equalizer pad, image zoom, the wallpaper wheel). They step once per event, which is a different thing from list distance.
- To change how far everything scrolls, change `Theme.wheelStep` (one number). A new scrolling list needs `WheelScroll { scroller: <id> }` next to it; `wheel_source_test.js` fails if one is missing.
- Files: `modules/common/Theme.qml` (`wheelPx`), `modules/common/widgets/WheelScroll.qml`, `modules/sekai/launcher/LauncherFace.qml`, `EmojiCard.qml`, `modules/sekai/wallpaperSelector/WallpaperFace.qml`, `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/CalendarCardWidget.qml`, `TodoWidget.qml`, `StickyNote.qml`, `PaletteWidget.qml`, `ReloadPopup.qml`, `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatSheet.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPage.qml`, `modules/sekai/editMode/EditThemeBoard.qml`, `EditPresetBoard.qml`, `EditTray.qml`, `modules/sekai/palette/PaletteTool.qml`, `modules/sekai/overview/Overview.qml`, `tests/wheel-test/wheel_source_test.js` (new).
- Undo: put the old files back from the previous zip.


## Unreleased — Palette is now a real desktop widget (a card that lives on the desktop)

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Brackets balanced in every edited file; every node test passes, and `palette_source_test.js` now also holds the new widget to the "looking never changes the theme" rule and checks it is wired in everywhere a desktop widget must be. **Nothing was drawn**, so the look of the card (the swatch size at your scale, the buttons on the pale card in dark mode) is the first thing to check.

- Fixed: **the Palette was only a pop-up tool, not a desktop widget.** Before, the shelf's Palette tile was a launcher ("OPEN") that closed Edit Desktop and opened the big tool. Now it is a normal tile ("ADD" / "EDIT"): the card sits on the desktop under your windows like Calendar and Weather, with drag, Lock all, per-monitor UI scale, monitors, Edit Desktop (move, remove, live picture on the shelf) and Desktop Focus Mode.
- The card: header (a chip says "Preview only" or "Applied"), **Wallpaper / Music / Random** tabs, five swatches (click = copy HEX, padlock or right-click = lock), **Shuffle / Save / Copy all**, **Apply to theme** (+ **Undo** when there is something to undo), and a row of favourites. The card only changes `PaletteService`'s own state; the theme changes only on **Apply to theme** (`PaletteService.apply()`, the one primary key), and Undo puts the old theme back. Also `toggle`, `hide`, `shuffle` (changes the strip only) and `resetPosition`. Place, monitor and on / off are remembered across restarts in the same `palette.json` the colours already use; an older `palette.json` keeps its colours and the card stays off.

Changed: `services/PaletteService.qml` (shown, place, monitor, `toolOpen`; saved in `palette.json`), `modules/sekai/palette/PaletteSwatch.qml` (a `compact` look; the tool keeps the big one), `modules/sekai/palette/PaletteTool.qml` (tells the service it is open), `shell.qml` (gate, loader, ipc `palettecard`), `services/EditMode.qml` and `modules/sekai/editMode/EditTraySlot.qml` (a real tile; the launcher code and `openPalette()` are gone), `modules/common/functions/FocusMath.js` and `FocusGate.qml` (wake-up order), `SettingsPageDesktop.qml` and `SettingsPageQuick.qml` (a switch each), `CheatBinds.js` (names), `tests/palette-test/palette_source_test.js`.

## Unreleased — Palette tool: colours from the wallpaper, music or luck, with favourites, HEX copy and a live preview

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no QML runtime here). Checked: `tests/palette-test/palette_unit.js` (the colour maths: picking five colours from a quantizer's list, seeded random harmonies with locks, the shell palette five colours make, favourites; 500+ palettes including all-black, all-white, all-grey and one-hue ones all keep the border's ink readable on both accents at contrast 4.5+ and white readable on the border at 5.2+), `tests/palette-test/palette_source_test.js` (reads the source to hold the tool to its one rule, below), every other existing node test still passes, `sh -n` on both scripts. Nothing was drawn, so **the look, the swatch lift and the colour glide are the things to check first**, then that the wallpaper / music read really fills the strip (`ColorQuantizer`, the same one the visualizer cover already uses).

- Added: **a Palette tool**, a card over the desktop (like the cheatsheet) with five big swatches, a live preview and your favourites.
  - **Wallpaper / Music / Random** tabs. Wallpaper reads the picture on screen (a video wallpaper gives its first frame); Music reads the cover of the playing track (a web cover is downloaded once into the same cache the visualizer uses); Random makes a colour-wheel harmony (analogous, complementary, split, triadic, tetradic or mono). A new wallpaper or the next song re-reads by itself while the card is open.
  - **Swatches**: click copies the HEX (`wl-copy`, a "Copied" tick shows on the swatch); the padlock (or right-click) locks a swatch so a shuffle or a new picture keeps its colour. Hover lifts a swatch off its shadow; colours and labels glide to the next colour instead of snapping. `Space` shuffles.
  - **Save** puts the palette in the favourites (click one to bring it back, the cross removes it; up to 48, remembered across restarts); **Copy all** copies the five HEX values.
  - **Live preview**: a mock-up of the shell (bar pill with workspace badges and clock chip, a card with buttons, two tiles) in the palette's colours and in your current Light / Dark / Darker mode, and a list of what the shell would wear (outline, accent, second accent, dark tone, hover wash).
- **The rule: looking never changes the theme.** The strip, the preview, shuffling, locking, favourites and copying only change the tool's own state (`PaletteService`). The preview draws with `PaletteMath.toShell(colors)` and never reads the live palette; a header chip says "Preview only, theme unchanged". The theme changes only when you press **Apply to theme**: the palette becomes a new **"Custom palette"** theme (it appears in Settings -> Quick -> Colours and the Edit Desktop theme row once there is one, is remembered across restarts, and goes through the same app-colour / system-theme sync as picking a group). **Undo** puts the theme you had back. `palette_source_test.js` fails if any drawing file names `ThemeService`, if anything but the Apply / Undo buttons can reach `applyPalette` / `setTheme`, or if the IPC can apply.
- **Apply gives what the preview showed**: both use the same `PaletteMath.toShell`. It takes the darkest colour as the outline (darkened until white text reads on it), the two most vivid / distinct as the accents (lifted until the outline's ink reads on them), and derives the rest (dark tone, hover wash, board, phone plastics) from those.
- Open it with `qs -c pjsekai ipc call palette toggle` (also `show`, `hide`, `shuffle`; `shuffle` only changes the strip), e.g. `bind = SUPER, P, exec, qs -c pjsekai ipc call palette toggle`, or **Settings -> Quick -> Look -> "Palette tool"**. Escape, the cross, a click outside or the keybind again closes it.
- Files: `modules/sekai/palette/` (`PaletteTool.qml`, `PaletteSwatch.qml`, `PalettePreview.qml`, `PaletteFavorite.qml`, `PaletteButton.qml`, `PaletteExtractor.qml`, all new), `modules/common/functions/PaletteMath.js` (new), `services/PaletteService.qml` (new), `scripts/state/palette-state.sh` (new; `~/.local/state/pjsekai/palette.json`), `services/ThemeService.qml` (the `custom` theme: `customColors`, `applyPalette()`, loaded before the saved theme id so a saved "custom" is accepted), `scripts/state/theme-state.sh` (`save-custom` / `load-custom`, `~/.local/state/pjsekai/custom-palette`), `shell.qml` (import + one line), `SettingsPageQuick.qml` (one button), `CheatBinds.js` (the `palette` IPC target is labelled), tests in `tests/palette-test/`.
- Not a desktop widget on purpose: it is an on-demand card like the cheatsheet, so it does not go through Edit Desktop, presets or Focus Mode. If you want it as a card that lives on the desktop too, the pieces (`PaletteSwatch`, `PalettePreview`, `PaletteService`) can be reused.
- Check on a live shell: open it (IPC), switch the three tabs and watch the strip change while the bar and everything else stays exactly as it was; shuffle and lock a swatch; click a swatch and paste somewhere; Save, close, reopen (the favourite and the strip should still be there, and opening must not replace them with the wallpaper's colours); Apply (the shell recolours, the chip turns green, "Custom palette" shows in the theme picker), then Undo; restart the shell with Custom applied and check it comes back.
- Known limits: colours cannot be typed in by hand yet (no hex entry box); the Music tab needs a player that gives cover art; the card is on the Overlay layer with the cheatsheet's keyboard grab, so opening it from the Settings phone may need the phone to give up focus first (if it stacks oddly, use the IPC bind). Undo: delete the `palette` folder, `PaletteMath.js`, `PaletteService.qml`, `palette-state.sh`, and put the old `ThemeService.qml`, `theme-state.sh`, `shell.qml`, `SettingsPageQuick.qml`, `CheatBinds.js` back.

## Unreleased — Calendar card: a month, today and what is coming up, as event banners (desktop widget)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no QML runtime here). Checked: the new `tests/calendar-test/events_unit.js` passes (dates, hand-typed times, what counts as an event, the "Coming up" order, deadline states, the chip words, the month grid's marks, and that `shell.qml`, Edit Desktop, the widget and the service line up); every existing unit test still passes; brackets balanced in every changed file; every `win.*` the widget uses is declared. Nothing was drawn, so **the look, the spacing at your scale, the typing into the form and the wheel paging are the things to check first**. The spec is a monthly overview, today, upcoming events, deadline indicators, month navigation, event details and highlighted event days.

- Added: **a desktop calendar card** (`CalendarCardWidget.qml`), 312 px wide, with the same drag / lock / scale / monitors / Edit Desktop / Focus Mode as the weather card. A plate with the month as a sticker, the year and week above it, and today as a tilted dark disc (click it to turn back to today); the month's tally on a slanted tab under the plate ("4 entries, 2 due", coral when a deadline has passed); two page keys, and the wheel over the days pages too.
- Added: **days that show what is on them.** A day with entries is washed with its first entry's colour and wears up to three diamonds (one per colour). A day with an open deadline gets a flag: coral when it is late or due today, amber within three days, grey further out. Click a day for just its entries (click again, or "All", to go back); double-click a day to add to it; a faded day from another month turns the page. Pointing at a day reads it out on the plate.
- Added: **"Coming up" as event banners** (`CalendarEventCard.qml`): a slanted block in the entry's colour with its weekday and day as a sticker, the title, the time and note, and a chip that counts down ("in 3 days", "Due tomorrow", "2 days late", "Done"). Open deadlines that have passed stay at the top until ticked off. Click a card for the full date, the whole note and Done / Undo (deadlines), Edit and Delete.
- Added: **an add / edit form** in place of the list (same height, so the card never jumps): title, note, time (24 h; `9:30`, `0930` and `9` work, empty = all day), Event or Deadline, one of the five attribute colours. The day is the one picked in the grid. Enter saves, Esc cancels.
- Added: `qs -c pjsekai ipc call calendarcard add "<title>" <YYYY-MM-DD> <HH:MM or ""> <event|deadline>` to add an entry from a script, besides `toggle`, `show`, `hide` and `resetPosition`.
- Added: a "Calendar" tile on the Edit Desktop shelf and a "Calendar card" switch in Settings -> Desktop and the Quick page.
- Off by default: the card does not appear on its own after an update; add it from the shelf, the Settings switch or `ipc call calendarcard show`.
- Entries are yours, like the To-Do's tasks: kept in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/calendarcard.json`, never expire, and a desktop preset does not carry them (or the card's position: presets do not know the card).
- Fixed on the way (in the new code only): two titles that differ only in letter case compared as "greater" both ways in the sort; caught by the test before it shipped.
- Layer namespace is `pjsekai-calendarcard`: if you have a Hyprland layer rule for `pjsekai-weathercard` (blur, ignorealpha), add the same for it.
- Files: `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/CalendarCardWidget.qml`, `CalendarEventCard.qml`, `CalendarChip.qml`, `CalendarField.qml`, `CalendarNavKey.qml` (all new); `modules/common/functions/EventMath.js` (new, all the rules); `services/CalendarCardService.qml`, `scripts/state/calendarcard-state.sh`, `tests/calendar-test/events_unit.js` (new). Changed: `shell.qml` (loader, gate, ipc), `services/EditMode.qml` (shelf entry), `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml` and `SettingsPageQuick.qml` (a switch each), `modules/common/functions/FocusMath.js` and `FocusGate.qml` (wake-up order, comment), `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatBinds.js` (names for the new ipc target).
- Not done: presets (the card is not in `DesktopPresetService` / `PresetMotion`, so wearing a preset neither moves nor hides it); reading a real calendar (ICS, khal, Google): entries are typed in or sent by `ipc`.
- Undo: delete the new files and revert the six edited ones.

## Unreleased — Sticky Notes: movable, resizable, colourful desktop notes that float or rest on the desktop

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no QML runtime here). Checked: the new `tests/notes-test/notes_unit.js` passes (keeping a note on screen, move and resize limits, the cascade of new notes, colours, the input-mask rectangle, reading a damaged save file), the existing node tests (focus, cheatsheet, grid, preset, keyboard, route, notify-kinds) still pass, `sh -n` and a save / load round trip of `notes-state.sh` with quotes and newlines in the text, bracket balance of every edited QML file. Nothing was drawn, so **the feel of dragging and resizing, and typing into a note, are the things to check first.**

- Added: **a "Sticky notes" desktop widget.** Any number of notes (up to 12), each a coloured paper in the shell's outlined-pill style with a hard shadow. Everything is saved: text, colour, place, size, pinned. A restart brings the notes back exactly as they were (`~/.local/state/pjsekai/notes.json`).
- Move: press and drag the **header strip** of a note. Resize: drag the **grip in the bottom-right corner**, or the right or bottom edge. A note never leaves the screen and never gets smaller than 200 x 130. Clicking or dragging a note brings it to the front.
- Edit: click the text and type. It is saved as you type (a moment after you stop). Esc leaves the text. A note keeps at most 3000 characters (the whole state is handed to the save script as one argument).
- Header keys, shown while the pointer is on the note (not while it is being moved or resized): **+** new note, **palette** colour (the keys swap for six swatches: yellow, pink, mint, cyan, lavender, peach), **pin**, **bin**. The bin deletes an empty note at once; a note with text asks once (the bin turns pink for 2.5 s, click again).
- **Controls and moving / resizing cannot fight**: the keys are only in the header, over the drag area; a press on a key is the key's, a press on the bare header starts a move. The resize zones are on the edges and the corner, clear of the header, the text and the keys. While a move or resize is going on the keys are hidden and switched off and the drag area keeps the pointer.
- **Pinned** = the note floats above your windows. Pinned notes are drawn by a second window on the Top layer (`pjsekai-notes-pinned`); the others are on the desktop under your windows like the other widgets (`pjsekai-notes`). Pinned notes ignore Desktop Focus Mode (that is the point), not Game Mode (all desktop widgets go then). Pinned notes can sit above or below the dock depending on the compositor's stacking.
- Edit Desktop: "Sticky Notes" is a tile on the shelf (click adds the notes, right-click hides them; hiding keeps the notes). While Edit Desktop is open every note is drawn by the desktop layer and shrinks into the card with the rest. Notes can still be moved, resized, recoloured, pinned and deleted there; the text can not be typed into (the shelf keeps the keyboard).
- Switches: Settings -> Desktop and Settings -> Quick have a "Sticky notes" toggle. Turning it on with no notes makes a first one; deleting the last note switches the widget off. `ipc call notes toggle | show | hide | create | arrange` (`create` = new note, `arrange` = every note back into a tidy cascade; `new` is a JS keyword so it can not be the name).
- Shell scale: positions are kept in design pixels and follow a changed scale like the other widgets (the note keeps its top-left spot on the screen). The notes live on the first monitor, like the To-Do.
- Not covered (left out to keep it small): the alignment grid / guides of Edit Desktop (they snap whole widgets, notes are free), the shelf's live picture of the widget (it shows the icon), desktop presets (wearing a preset does not move or hide notes), notes on other monitors, rich text.
- Files: `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/StickyNote.qml`, `StickyNotesWidget.qml`, `services/NotesService.qml`, `scripts/state/notes-state.sh`, `modules/common/functions/NotesMath.js`, `tests/notes-test/notes_unit.js` (all new); `shell.qml` (gate, two loaders, IPC), `services/EditMode.qml` (shelf entry), `modules/common/functions/FocusMath.js` and `FocusGate.qml` (wake-up slot, comment), `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `SettingsPageQuick.qml` (toggle), `CheatBinds.js` (names for the cheatsheet). Undo: put the edited files back and delete the new ones (the saved notes stay in `notes.json`).
- Check on a live shell: Settings -> Desktop -> Sticky notes on: a yellow note should pop in near the top right. Drag it by the header; drag the corner; click the text and type, restart the shell and look for the text. Open the palette and pick a colour. Pin it and open a window over it: it should stay above. Unpin: it goes under the window. Make a few (+) and check the colours rotate and the pile cascades. Bin on a note with text needs two clicks. Open Edit Desktop (right-click the desktop): the notes shrink with the card and the shelf has a Sticky Notes tile. Click the desktop between two notes: it must go to the desktop (right-click opens the menu). If a note's keyboard does not work on the Top layer, tell me what Hyprland does when you click it.
- If Hyprland animates the windows oddly, add `hl.layer_rule({ match = { namespace = "^pjsekai-notes" }, no_anim = true })` to `hyprland.lua`.

## Unreleased — Custom Image widget: your own pictures (and GIFs) anywhere on the desktop

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no QML runtime here). Checked: `tests/image-test/image_unit.js` passes (the sizing / crop / resize maths run for real, and the QML wiring is read from source); the other node tests still pass; bracket balance of every new QML file; `sh -n` and a sandbox run of both new scripts (copy, GIF kept byte for byte, remove only inside the data folder, chooser). Nothing was drawn, so **the first things to check are: the shape of a rounded GIF, the soft shadow's blur size, and where the panel lands next to a picture.**

- Fixed: **the picture showed past the border's outer edge.** The picture and the stroke both ended on the shape's edge, so the picture's antialiased rounded mask (or a half-covered pixel of a plain cut) peeked out as a thin fringe around the stroke. With a border on, the picture and its mask are now pulled in by the stroke's width less one pixel (that pixel runs under the stroke, so no gap), with the corner radius reduced to match; FIT covers that inner area so a thick border never squeezes the picture out of shape, and the crop pan uses it too. No border: nothing changes. Files: `ImageWidget.qml`, `tests/image-test/image_unit.js`.
- Added: **Custom Image**, as many pictures as you like, each its own window like the other desktop widgets (under your windows, over the wallpaper, click-through everywhere but the picture). Add one from the shelf's **Image** tile in Edit Desktop (a file chooser opens, the new picture is selected), from Settings -> Desktop -> Widgets -> Add an image, or `qs -c pjsekai ipc call image pick` / `image addPath <file>`.
- Added: **resizing that keeps the aspect ratio.** Select a picture in Edit Desktop and drag a corner diamond; the opposite corner stays put and the frame can't leave the screen. Shift while dragging flips the panel's LOCK RATIO for that drag; ORIGINAL RATIO gives the frame the picture's own ratio back.
- Added: **FIT / FILL / CROP.** FIT shows the whole picture and the border, rounding and shadow follow the picture (no empty bars inside them). FILL covers the frame, centred. CROP covers it with the part you choose: drag the picture to pan, scroll to zoom (up to 6x); a grip on the frame's top-left corner moves the frame meanwhile.
- Added: **a panel beside the selected picture** (`ImageInspector.qml`): change / copy / delete the picture, ROUND (0-100% of the roundest the shape allows, so a circle is possible and it stays round when resized), OPACITY (5-100%, the border does not show the picture through itself), BORDER (width 1-24 px and navy / white / mint / cyan / pink / black, which follow the theme), SHADOW (soft, or the shell's hard navy sticker shadow, and how far it reaches). It is drawn at full size, not shrunk with Edit Desktop's card.
- Added: **GIF, animated WebP and APNG.** The file is copied as it is (never re-encoded or shrunk, which would stop it moving) and plays only while the picture is on screen: it pauses with Focus Mode, Game Mode and when the widget is switched off. Still pictures are decoded no bigger than the frame needs (a 12000 px photo does not sit in memory at full size); phone photos are turned upright from their EXIF orientation.
- Added: **everything is remembered per picture**: file, spot, monitor, size, mode, crop, rounding, opacity, border, shadow, ratio lock, in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/images.json`; the copies are in `~/.local/share/pjsekai/custom-images/`. A position or size is saved the moment you drop it, the rest 300 ms after the last change. A picture whose file has gone shows a "picture missing" frame you can replace or delete. Deleting a picture deletes its copy (but not while another picture, a duplicate, still uses it).
- Added: `image toggle | show | hide | pick | addPath <file> | list | resetPosition` over IPC; the cheat sheet knows them.
- Not done: **desktop presets do not carry the pictures.** A preset saves the avatar and banner by copying them; doing the same for any number of pictures (and GIFs) is a separate piece of work, so wearing a preset leaves your pictures as they are.
- Files: `modules/sekai/desktopWidgets/ImageWidget.qml`, `ImageInspector.qml`, `modules/common/functions/ImageMath.js`, `services/ImageService.qml`, `scripts/state/image-state.sh`, `scripts/images/pick-custom-image.sh`, `tests/image-test/image_unit.js` (all new); `shell.qml` (the Variants, the IPC), `services/EditMode.qml` (the shelf tile, `image:<id>` ids), `modules/sekai/editMode/EditTray.qml` (title), `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `modules/sekai/cheatSheet/CheatBinds.js`, `modules/common/functions/FocusMath.js` / `FocusGate.qml` (an `image` wake-up slot). Undo: delete the new files and revert those edits.

## Unreleased — Discord call popup: the wheel layer no longer sits inside the member list's Column

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** Checked: bracket balance of `DiscordPopup.qml`, and that no other `WheelScroll` in the shell sits inside a Column / Row (the Discord popup was the only one).

- Fixed: the to-do pass's one wheel feel (`WheelScroll.qml`) was put straight into the popup's Column, next to the member list. A Column lays its children out itself, so the wheel layer took a slot of its own (an extra gap in the card) and the Column rejects the `anchors.fill` it uses. The list and the wheel layer now sit together in a plain Item the size of the list, as in every other list.

## Unreleased — Overview cork board: a vertical column (left / center / right), 1-10 workspaces; 1-10 workspaces on the bar

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt 6 / QML runtime here). Checked: bracket balance of every changed file, and the layout maths of the board (card size, column height, when it scrolls) worked out for 1536x864, 1920x1080 and 2560x1440 with 1, 3, 5, 8 and 10 workspaces. **Nothing was drawn, so open the Overview first: the board's position, the scroll and dragging a window between cards are the things to look at.**

- Changed: **the Overview cork board is now vertical**: one tall column of workspace cards (the cards used to sit in a row). Each card is an equal share of the screen's height, never smaller than a mock screen 88 px tall; the board is centred in the space the bar leaves free.
- Added: **Board position: Left / Center / Right** (Settings -> Hyprland -> Overview, `overview.position`, default center). On the left or right it keeps 24 px from the edge (or from the bar, if the bar is on that side) and slides in from that edge as it fades in.
- Added: **Workspaces shown, 1-10** (same card, `overview.workspaces`, default 5). Windows on workspaces past the number are not drawn, as before for anything past 5.
- Added: **the column scrolls when it does not fit** (a lot of workspaces on a small screen): mouse wheel / touchpad (the shell's one wheel feel, `Theme.wheelStep`), Up / Down (one card), Page Up / Page Down (three), and a thin marker on the cork. It opens with the workspace you are on in view. Dragging a window onto a card that is scrolled out of sight is not possible: scroll first, then drag.
- Added: **Workspaces on the bar, 1-10** (Settings -> Bar -> Modules, `bar.workspaceCount`, default 5). The badge row (or column on a standing bar) grows with it; the wheel over it stops at the last one. The colours repeat every five.
- Only for the cork board: the Scroll (niri) overview draws its own strip, so the two Overview settings are hidden while it is picked. Hyprland needs binds for the extra workspaces (SUPER+6 .. 0) if you want keys for them; clicking a card or a badge switches to it either way.
- Fixed: **on a standing bar the workspaces pill vanished with many workspaces.** The middle zone of a standing bar drops itself when it does not fit between the top and bottom zones, and with 10 badges it never did, so the badges went with it. The column now only draws the badges around the active workspace, as many as fit (never fewer than 3), and the window follows the active workspace as you click or scroll (`WorkspaceBadges.maxLength` / `shown` / `first`, sized by `Status_Bar.badgesRoom` from what the other zones leave free). A flat bar is unchanged. Files: `WorkspaceBadges.qml`, `Status_Bar.qml`. Not run: **check it on a standing bar with 10 workspaces first.** If even 3 badges do not fit next to everything else, the middle zone still drops itself, as it always did.
- The old row layout is gone (no switch for it). Undo: put the old `Overview.qml` back.
- Files: `modules/sekai/overview/Overview.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/Bar.qml`, `modules/sekai/bar/Status_Bar.qml` (`workspaceCount`), `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageHyprland.qml`, `modules/sekai/settings/SettingsPageBar.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml` (the three options).

## Unreleased — Welcome logo: rebuilt as separately animated layers, a burst out of the middle, and a collapse back into it

**Not run on a live Quickshell.** This time the QML *was* run, though: the logo was rendered frame by frame with a real Qt 6.12 (PySide6, OpenGL on a software rasteriser, real `MultiEffect` / `ShaderEffect` / compiled `.qsb`) in a stub of the shell's `Theme`, with **Poppins standing in for the shell's font** (no variable `ROND` axis), and compared against the reference video frame by frame. `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js` passes (175 checks). Not measured: speed on a real GPU, and the real font's outline and spacing. **Run `./shaders/compile-shaders.sh` first** (the `.qsb` files shipped here were built with Qt 6.12's `qsb`; build them with your own so they match your Qt), then restart the shell; check the real font and the speed first.

- Fixed (a real bug, not taste): **every hidden layer in the logo went stale.** `visible: false` + `layer.enabled` is not redrawn when something inside it changes while it is hidden, so the letters' mask and colour block (and anything grown inside them at runtime) did not keep up. They are now `ShaderEffectSource { hideSource: true; visible: false }`, which is what Qt documents for this.
- Changed: **the entrance is a burst out of the middle, not a rise from below.** A small ignition at the centre (a glow, a ring and a four-point flash), then every letter leaves the middle at scale 0 on its own curved line, hops past its place and size, swings through upright and settles crooked. Order is k, e, a, S, o, i, P, p (not left to right); each slot has its own start, length, spring (damping and speed, so a different hop each, 13-20%), start angle and flight bend (`WelcomeMath.js` `BURST`). The kana (now **hiragana** せかい; `kana` in `WelcomeLogo.qml` has the katakana if you want it back) come in on arcs while the last letters land.
- Changed: **each letter is built from layers that come in one after the other.** Gradient rim (light at the top, deep below; cut from one picture of the string, so it is smooth, no more stairs), a solid extrusion (the same picture shown several times, no gaps), two flowing wave bands, and the halftone as a shader (`shaders/welcome_dots.frag`): a shading in the lower right that stays, a matte band that crosses the letter and swells the dots under it, and a lattice that drifts and loops. A hard shine sweeps last. Idle: the shine, the matte and the waves keep going.
- Added: **the combined pass** over the whole word. The light outer stroke (thicker than before) comes up behind the letters with a halftone of its own that a matte crosses; then a travelling wave warps the whole assembled logo (stroke and all) while a light sweep crosses it (`shaders/welcome_warp.vert` / `.frag`: the logo is drawn into a texture and shown on a grid whose points the vertex shader moves). The individual letter effects stay under it.
- Added: **accents with their own entrances**: four sparkles that come out of the middle a short way (their own spring, a quarter turn), a squiggle that draws itself on, all going back the same way on exit. New: `WelcomeIgnition.qml`, `WelcomeSquiggle.qml`.
- Changed: **the exit collapses everything into the middle.** The kana and sparkles go first, then the letters in the reverse order of the entrance; each draws a short breath outward, then is pulled in while it shrinks, spins and goes out of focus; the stroke shrinks away and a small flash closes it. 560 ms (`LOGO_LEAVE_MS`); the page no longer fades or scales the logo as one piece on the way out (`WelcomePage.qml`, `leaveMs` in `WelcomeSlides.js`).
- Changed: the run is 3.2 s (was 2.8) so the wave and the sweep have room, and the second line ("Pop") is now in front of the first (its P was being covered).
- Without the `.qsb` files the logo still comes in whole: the wave and sweep are skipped and the letters keep still dots (tested by deleting them).
- Files: `modules/sekai/welcome/` (`WelcomeLetter`, `WelcomePopLetter`, `WelcomeLogo`, `WelcomePage`, new `WelcomeIgnition`, `WelcomeSquiggle`), `modules/common/functions/WelcomeMath.js` (a whole timeline in ms, `springEx`, `BURST`, the exit), `WelcomeSlides.js`, `shaders/welcome_dots.frag`, `welcome_warp.vert`, `welcome_warp.frag` (+ `.qsb`), `tests/welcome-test/welcome_unit.js`. Undo: put the old files back and delete the three shaders.

## Unreleased — Screen Snip: a visible key for every tool

## Unreleased — To-do pass: smoother tape, Active window text and standing-bar key, lockscreen media, EQ shadow

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt 6 here). Only bracket balance of every changed file was checked against the original, so **nothing below has been drawn: look at each first.**

- Fixed: **tape edges are smooth** (`WashiTape.qml`). The strip was a canvas exactly the size of the tape, so as soon as it was rotated its edges were hard and stepped and the torn ends looked like saw teeth. It is now drawn 4 times larger (`quality`) inside a 2 px transparent margin (`pad`) and shown scaled back down, and the points of the torn ends are rounded a little (`softness: 1.4`; set 0 for the old sharp points). The same file serves the Edit Desktop shelf, the cheatsheet, the launcher cards, the overview and the wallpaper wheel. Undo: put the old `WashiTape.qml` back.
- Fixed: **Active window with a long or symbol-heavy title** (`Status_Bar.qml`). A title with an emoji, symbol or CJK in it (a Discord channel, a browser tab) is drawn with a fallback font whose lines are taller; that made the title line taller, the centred block grew, the app name slid up against the top edge and the title dropped. Each line now has a box of its own with a fixed line height (1.25 x the font size), one line, plain text, newlines turned into spaces; a long title is cut with "...". Normal titles should look the same.
- Added: **Active window on a standing bar** (new `ActiveWindowKey.qml`, `Status_Bar.qml`, `Bar.qml`). A square key the size of the other square keys: the focused app's icon (its first letter if no icon is found, the workspace number while nothing is focused), and "App - title" in the hover tag. `Bar.qml` resolves the icon from the window's class (`windowIconSource`) and passes it as `windowIcon`. No click action yet. Undo: delete `ActiveWindowKey.qml` and the `activeWindowKey` block, `windowIcon` property and `windowIconSource`.
- Fixed: **lockscreen media comes and goes smoothly** (`LockSurface.qml`). The rule and the now-playing row were `visible: media.present`, so the panel jumped 126 px the moment a player appeared or went away and the text blanked first. The block now opens and closes (320 ms, height then opacity) and keeps the last title / artist on screen while it closes. A player already there when the screen locks is just there (no opening).
- Fixed: **EQ widget shadow** (`PadBody.qml`). The 18 shadow copies all started from the same corner and only grew down and to the right, so the shadow was lopsided and ran past the popup's window, where it was cut off in a straight line. It is now the footprint of the wall's far end plus 12 copies growing evenly on every side, never more than 9 design px (`shadowSpread`) past the wall, so it fades out evenly and stays inside the popup. A bigger, softer shadow needs more room: raise `shadowSpread` together with `marginR` / `marginB` in `EqualizerPopup.qml`.
- Not done yet: the new lockscreen design and the unmapped keybinds (both need a decision / your Hyprland binds file).
