## Unreleased — clipboard: the picture and the text now match the row you picked

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Checked: the real `ClipboardCard.qml` loaded in Qt 6 (headless, stubbed theme) and driven the way `ExtrasSource.qml` drives it, before and after; `qmllint` finds no syntax errors in the edited files; the picture-decode script run for real against a fake `cliphist` (png, jpeg, and webp turned into png).

- Fixed: **the sheet showed the entry one step behind the highlighted row, and the newest entry never loaded.** `onEntriesChanged` / `onCurrentChanged` in `ClipboardCard.qml` read `currentEntry`, but inside a change handler that binding has not been re-evaluated yet, so it still held the previous entry (or `null`). Reproduced: when the list arrived the card reported `null` (newest entry blank; a copied picture never appeared), moving Down to row 8 loaded row 9, and a new copy landing on top made the selection drift to another row. The handlers now read `entries[current]` directly (`entryAt()`), and `activate()` does too.
- Fixed: **a pick made while a decode was still running was dropped.** A Quickshell `Process` stays "running" until it has really exited, so `running = false; running = true` did nothing and the new entry stayed on "Loading" until the 4 s timer said it failed. `ExtrasSource.selectClip` now keeps the latest pick, stops the old decode, and starts the new one from `onRunningChanged` (latest pick wins).
- Changed: a failed decode is reported **when the process ends** without an answer (the 4 s timer is now a 10 s backstop, so a big picture being converted is not called failed), and the card shows what `cliphist` / `magick` printed under "Could not load this picture" (`clipError`).
- Changed: the dock's Clipboard card names a picture like the launcher does (`Image · 1.2 MiB png 1920x1080`) and puts it back on the clipboard with its own type.
- Files: `ClipboardCard.qml`, `ExtrasSource.qml`, `LauncherExtras.qml` (passes `error`), `DockClipboardCard.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — rename a preset

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here), so how the tile looks and feels is untested. Checked: brace balance of the edited QML files; `services/preset-rename-test/rename_unit.js` (new) runs `preset-state.sh rename` for real in a temp folder (file and pictures move together, the name inside changes, a taken name is refused with nothing touched, bad names, capitals-only, a stray pictures folder doesn't come along, the pictures still restore under the new name), runs `rename()` from the service against fakes, and checks the board's wiring; the other preset tests still pass.

- New: **a pencil on every preset tile** (top left of its picture, on hover, next to SAVE OVER). One press turns the name under the picture into a field with the name selected: **Enter** keeps it, **Esc** cancels. A click on the rest of the tile while typing does nothing (it doesn't wear the preset). Leaving the tab drops a half-typed name.
- Refused names turn the field red and leave it open: an **empty** name, or one **another preset already has** (capitals ignored, so `night` is refused when `Night` exists). Renaming never adds " 2" the way saving does, since that would not be the name you typed. The same name, or only its capitals changed (`night` to `Night`), is fine.
- The name is cleaned like a new preset's: no slashes or control characters, no leading dots, one space between words, 40 characters. The tile changes at once and the list is read again when the file is done moving.
- What moves: the preset's file, its pictures folder (`<name>.images/`, the avatar and banner it keeps) and the `_meta.name` saved inside it. Nothing else in the preset is touched, and its saved time stays. The tick on a tile, and a preset being worn right now, follow the new name.
- New: `qs -c pjsekai ipc call presets rename "Night desk" "Late desk"` (prints the name it ended up with, nothing when it was refused).
- `services/preset-state.sh rename <old> <new> <json>`: refuses with exit 3 when `<new>` is another preset (nothing changed), 1 when `<old>` isn't there, 2 for a bad name; a failure half way puts the pictures folder back.
- Files: `EditPresetBoard.qml`, `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (`rename`, `byName`, IPC), `services/preset-state.sh`, `services/preset-rename-test/rename_unit.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — window borders that follow the theme (Settings -> Desktop -> App colours -> Window borders)

**Not run on a live Hyprland** (none here). Checked: `sh -n services/app-theme.sh`, brace balance of the edited .qml files.

- New: **Window borders**, a fifth switch in App colours (`appTheme.hyprland`, target `hyprland` in `app-theme.sh`): Hyprland's `general.col.active_border` is the theme's accent and `general.col.inactive_border` its muted colour, so they follow the theme, the light / dark mode and the matugen wallpaper colours, like the other app switches (rewritten a moment after a change). Applied live with `hyprctl eval`, and kept as one line tagged `-- pjsekai:apptheme:hyprland` in `~/.config/hypr/pjsekai_overrides.lua` (hyprland.lua has to load it, see the Hyprland page's one-time setup). **Revert** removes that line and reloads Hyprland, which gives back the colours of your own `hyprland.lua`.
- Colours: the active border is the theme's accent pushed to a stronger colour of the same hue, and the inactive one is that hue darker, not the grey mix of text and background an earlier build used (which looked grey with every wallpaper). Sent to the script as `hlactive` / `hlinactive`.
- Not changed: the border thickness (Settings -> Hyprland -> Gaps & borders; `0` means no border at all), so a border has to be on for the colours to show. The ScrollOverview plugin draws windows with their Hyprland borders, so they show there too (not confirmed against the plugin's source).
- Files: `services/app-theme.sh`, `services/AppThemeService.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — an option to use the ScrollOverview plugin (niri-style) as the Overview

**Not run on a live Quickshell or Hyprland** (none here). Checked: brace balance of the edited files, `sh -n` on `hyprland-settings.sh`. Needs a real test, see below.

- New: **Settings -> Hyprland -> Overview -> Overview style**: `Cork board` (the Overview this shell always had, still the default) or `Scroll (niri)`, the Hyprland plugin https://github.com/yayuuu/hyprland-scroll-overview (`overview.style` in `SettingsService`, `board` | `scroll`). With `Scroll` picked, `ipc call overview toggle | show | hide` and the dock's Search-key fallback drive the plugin instead (`scrolloverview:overview toggle` / `on` / `off`, or `hl.plugin.scrolloverview.overview("toggle")` on a Lua config), so the key you already bound keeps working and nothing in `hyprland.lua` has to change. The plugin draws the overview itself (the compositor), so its look is the plugin's, not this shell's.
- Auto-load: with `Scroll` picked and the plugin not loaded (at start-up, right after picking it, on **Check again**, or on the first overview key press), the shell runs `hyprpm reload -n` once itself, so no `hl.on("hyprland.start", ...)` line is needed in `hyprland.lua`. The plugin still has to be installed and enabled with hyprpm once. When it still did not load, you are told: a notification ("Scroll overview did not start", once per try, with what `hyprpm` said and whether the cork board opened instead) and the same text on the Settings card.
- Safe fallback: if `Scroll` is picked but the plugin is not loaded (not installed, or hyprpm has not loaded it yet), the cork board opens instead, so the key never does nothing. The check is `hyprctl plugin list`, done at start-up, when the option changes, when the card is built, on **Check again**, and only while the plugin is not known to be loaded, right before an open.
- New: the card shows whether the plugin was found; when it was not, it shows the install line (`hyprpm add ... && hyprpm update && hyprpm enable scrolloverview`) with a **Copy the commands** button. When it is loaded: **Workspaces stack** (vertical / sideways), **Zoom out** (`scale`, 0.1 to 0.9) and **Gap between workspaces**. These are kept in the shell's own settings (`overview.layout` / `.scale` / `.gap`; unset leaves your `hyprland.lua` plugin config alone) and applied live with `hyprctl eval` once the plugin is loaded and after every config reload (`configreloaded`).
- Fixed (same release): an earlier build of this feature wrote those options to `~/.config/hypr/pjsekai_overrides.lua`, which runs before hyprpm has loaded the plugin, so Hyprland showed "unknown config key 'plugin.scrolloverview.workspace_gap'" on every reload. Nothing about the plugin goes in that file any more; if you ran that build, delete the leftover lines: `sed -i '/plugin\.scrolloverview/d' ~/.config/hypr/pjsekai_overrides.lua`.
- New: `ipc call scrolloverview toggle | show | hide | status` (drives the plugin directly) and `ipc call overview style [scroll|board]` (reads or switches the option).
- Changed: presets leave `overview.style` alone (`keepPrefix`), like the other personal switches. Edit Desktop starting also hides the plugin's overview if it is up.
- Not changed: the cork board, its tiles and its gestures; they are used exactly as before when `Cork board` is picked (and as the fallback).
- To test: pick `Scroll (niri)`, press your overview key (plugin loaded: the plugin's overview; not loaded: the cork board). `qs -c pjsekai ipc call scrolloverview status`. Move Zoom out and Gap, then open it. Lua config only: confirm the dispatch string `hl.plugin.scrolloverview.overview("toggle")` takes effect through `Hyprland.dispatch` (the same path `hl.dsp.focus(...)` uses elsewhere); if it does nothing, tell me what `hyprctl dispatch` prints.
- Files: `Overview.qml`, `SettingsPageHyprland.qml`, `services/ScrollOverviewService.qml` (new), `services/SettingsService.qml`, `services/HyprlandSettings.qml` (read-only paths for the plugin's live values), `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Apps launcher: no Home, a clean slide instead of the bounce

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Checked: brace, bracket and parenthesis balance of `LauncherFace.qml` and `QuickPanel.qml`, no leftover references to what was removed.

- Removed: **Home.** The Apps screen is the A to Z grid and nothing else: the Home page, its pinned apps, the Home / All apps button at the bottom, the Back chip, and the Settings and Wallpapers tiles are all gone (Settings is the gear on the Quick Panel; the Wallpapers keep their key and `ipc call wallpaperpicker toggle`). The grid uses the room the button had. Escape clears the search, then goes back.
- Changed: **the phone's motion on the Apps screen** (`launching` in `QuickPanel.qml`): a slide, no bounce, softer than the first try. The lift runs 340 ms (`launchInMs`) on a sheet-style Bezier curve (`launchCurve`: gentle start, speeds up, long soft stop) instead of the hard `OutQuart` that made it stiff; the tilt straightens a little behind it (460 ms, `launchTiltMs`) so the phone settles into place instead of riding up as one block. The close (fixed from a screen recording: it waited about 40 ms before it moved, then jumped into a fast, even drop with the tilt on a different timing): it now starts at once, with no redraw wait, and the lift and the tilt go together on one curve (`launchOutCurve`, 300 ms `launchOutMs`) that gathers speed and ends softly. No pull-up. The Quick Panel keeps its bounce and its wait.
- New: **the grid arrives with the phone**: it follows a beat behind the phone (60 ms), rising 30 units, fading and growing from 96% over 320 ms every time the screen comes up (`revealT`). Tile hover and press are a touch smoother. The keyboard is still handed to the search box after 20 ms, and the app icons load asynchronously.
- Tune: `launchInMs` / `launchTiltMs` / `launchOutMs`, `launchCurve` and `launchOutCurve` in `QuickPanel.qml`; `revealAnim` in `LauncherFace.qml`.
- Files: `LauncherFace.qml`, `QuickPanel.qml`, `LauncherPhone.qml` (its `onBuiltinActivated` line: the signal is gone, and it stopped the shell loading), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — vertical visualizer bands

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here). Checked: the QML files' brackets balance, and a mock desktop was rendered by turning two of the shaders' CPU-simulated frames on their sides the same way the widget does (`vertical_preview.png`).

- New: **a vertical option for the band visualizers** (Bars, Aurora, Dots, Mirror, Tide, Ribbons, Scope, Pulse; the Sekai card and the Ring have no direction). Settings > Desktop > **Visualizer direction** (Horizontal / Vertical), a new **rotate button** on the band's corner buttons in Edit Desktop (next to style and size), or `qs -c pjsekai ipc call visualizer vertical true` (also `vertical false`, `toggleVertical`).
- How it looks: the band runs the full height of the screen, as wide as the size you picked (S / M / L / XL), up the **right edge** by default with its base on the edge and the bass at the bottom. Drag it across in Edit Desktop (it moves sideways instead of up and down) and, when you drop it nearer the left half, it turns to face the left edge (base on the left, bass still at the bottom). It does not flip while you are dragging.
- How it works: the band is drawn exactly as before and the widget turns it 90 degrees (`rotor` / `flip` in `VisualizerWidget.qml`), so every style works with no change to its shader and costs the same. The window around it is the thin strip it needs, as before.
- Each direction remembers its own spot: `bandY` for the bottom band, the new `bandX` for the vertical one, so switching back and forth keeps both. `ipc call visualizer resetPosition` resets both.
- Presets carry it for free: a desktop preset saves the visualizer's whole saved state, which now includes `vertical` and `bandX`, and the card glides to the preset's spot (`follow`, `onBandXChanged`, `onVerticalChanged` in `VisualizerWidget.qml`).
- Files: `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `services/VisualizerService.qml`, `services/EditMode.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `shell.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — wearing a preset moves things instead of jumping

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here), so the timings and how it feels are untested. Checked: brace balance of every edited QML file; `services/preset-motion-test/preset_motion_unit.js` (new: which parts a preset changes, the slide-away-then-wear order, and the wiring in `Bar.qml`, `Dock.qml` and the five widgets) passes, and the two earlier preset tests still pass.

- New: **the bar and the dock slide off their edge, the preset goes on, and they slide back in.** Only the ones the preset really changes move: the bar when its style, edge, left / centre / right arrangement or any `bar.*` switch differs, the dock when any `dock.*` setting differs (the Files key's command is ignored, as always). A preset that changes neither is worn at once, as before. Slide away is 190 ms, slide back 380 ms with a small overshoot (`PresetMotion.outMs` / `inMs`).
- A new bar or dock edge builds a new window (see `shell.qml`). That window is created while the swap is still running, starts off its edge and slides in with the rest, so a moved bar or dock arrives instead of popping up. `PresetMotion.barBusy` / `dockBusy` stay true for 2 s after the slide back for that.
- New: **desktop widgets glide to their new spot** (clock, user card, weather, visualizer, to-do): about half a second with a little overshoot. While gliding, a widget whose window was shrunk to the card (`compact`) gets the whole screen back, then shrinks again. The clock, visualizer and to-do give their usual small landing bump at the end. A card that is already where the preset puts it does not move, and a card you are dragging is never touched. Showing or hiding a widget keeps its existing pop-in / swallow, and a widget moved to another monitor still jumps (its window changes screen).
- New: **a widget that changes variant shrinks away and the new one pops back in.** Same sequence as the bar and the dock: the card shrinks to 70 % and fades out (190 ms), the preset goes on, the new variant comes back with a small overshoot (380 ms). Covers the **clock's design** (Keys, Tower, Sticker, Stack) and the **visualizer's style, colours, size and direction** (a band along the bottom or up the side). The card's usual small bump and the "DESIGN / STYLE / SIZE" label still show afterwards. Not when only its position changes (it glides), and not for an older preset that holds no value for the key.
- New: **a widget the preset switches off shrinks away with the rest**, instead of being swallowed after the bar and dock have already moved. All five cards do it (clock, user card, weather, visualizer, to-do), also the ones with no variants. A card stays away until its own swallow is over (`swapRest`, 420 ms), so it can't come back for a moment while it is still shrinking out. A widget the preset switches **on** keeps its pop-in, one that is off before and after does nothing, and a band-style visualizer fades instead of shrinking, as it always has (it spans the screen).
- While a swap is running (about 0.4 s plus the slide back) another `presets apply` is ignored, like it is while the wallpaper files are being checked.
- Not done: the wallpaper already has its own transition and is unchanged; the tiles on the PRESETS tab do not animate.
- Tuning: durations are `outMs` / `inMs` in `PresetMotion.qml` and `duration: 520` in the widgets' `Behavior on x / y`; how far the dock goes is `fullHide` in `Dock.qml`, the bar's is `slideDist` in `Bar.qml`.
- Files: `PresetMotion.qml` (new; `clockOut` / `visualizerOut` too), `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (`motionPlan`, `finishApply`, `wear`), `Bar.qml`, `Dock.qml`, `ClockWidget.qml` and `VisualizerWidget.qml` (`swapK`), `UserCardWidget.qml`, `WeatherCardWidget.qml`, `TodoWidget.qml`, `services/preset-motion-test/preset_motion_unit.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — desktop widgets move to the preset's spot

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here). Checked: brace balance of the five edited widgets, and `services/preset-widgets-test/widgets_follow_unit.js` (new, reads the sources) passes.

- Fixed: **wearing a preset did not move the desktop widgets** (clock, user card, weather, visualizer, to-do). A preset saved each widget's spot and handed it back to the widget's service, but the widget only looked at the service when it loaded or when its window, scale or monitor changed, so the card stayed where it was until the next restart. Each widget now places itself again when the service's `posX`, `posY` or `posScale` change (the visualizer also for `bandY` and when it switches to or from a band style).
- The spot arrives as several properties one after the other, so the widget waits 16 ms and places once (a `follow` timer), instead of jumping to a half-updated spot. It does not place while the card is being dragged, so moving a card by hand is unchanged.
- Dropping a card on the desktop still saves its spot the same way; the new handler only places the card where the service already says it is.
- A preset still moves a widget to another monitor the way it always did (the saved monitor name changes the window's screen).
- Files: `ClockWidget.qml`, `UserCardWidget.qml`, `WeatherCardWidget.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `TodoWidget.qml`, `services/preset-widgets-test/widgets_follow_unit.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — presets really keep the avatar and banner

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here). Checked: `preset-state.sh` save and restore run for real with two presets holding different pictures (wearing A puts A's avatar and banner back, wearing B puts B's back, `pick-image.sh load` finds the worn ones), `preset_images_unit.js` passes (now also checks the import below).

- Fixed: **presets did not keep the avatar and banner.** `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` uses `PanelImages`, but that file sits in the config root, which is a different QML module from `services/`. The service never imported it, so `PanelImages` was undefined when the service ran: capturing the desktop failed before the pictures were looked at, so nothing about them was saved or put back. Added `import qs`. Every other file that uses `PanelImages` is in the root, which is why nothing else was affected.
- If a preset still holds no pictures after this, save it again (the SAVE tile, or overwrite from its menu): presets saved while the import was missing have no `images` part, and wearing one leaves your avatar and banner alone by design.
- Files: `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`, `services/preset-images-test/preset_images_unit.js`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — picking an avatar or banner no longer stalls the desktop

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here), so the cause below is a reasoned one, not a measured one. Checked: `shrink-image.sh` on made-up pictures with ImageMagick, with ffmpeg alone and with no tools (`services/preset-images-test/shrink_test.sh`, passes); `pick-image.sh` run for real with stand-in choosers (the picture it prints is the shrunk one, the original is untouched, `load` still finds it, `PJSEKAI_CHOOSER` picks the chooser and ignores junk values); `preset-state.sh restore` on a big saved copy.

- Fixed (most likely cause): **the desktop stuttered for a moment after picking a big picture.** `pick-image.sh` copied the file as it was, and the shell then decoded that same file again for every place that shows it (Quick Panel banner, Quick Panel avatar, the user card's banner and avatar, once per monitor). A PNG or WebP has to be decoded in full before Qt can scale it, so a 24-megapixel photo meant several full decodes and a few hundred MB at once. **The picture is now scaled down once, while it is copied** (`shrink-image.sh`, new): the avatar to cover 800 x 800, the banner to cover 2000 x 800, which is the largest slot at 2x with room to spare, and the part the slots crop keeps its detail. Anything not bigger than that both ways is copied byte for byte; svg and gif are always copied as they are. It runs at the lowest CPU and disk priority, in its own process. ImageMagick (magick / convert), else ffmpeg + ffprobe, else a plain copy; if scaling fails for any reason the picture is copied as it is, picking never fails because of it.
- The same scaling is applied when a **preset puts its avatar / banner back**, so presets saved earlier from a big picture are scaled when you wear them. The preset's own copy and your original file are not changed.
- Cost: for a very big, noisy PNG the scaling can take a second or two (about 2 s for a 24 MP, 119 MB test PNG on this machine), so the new picture appears that much later. A normal photo is a fraction of that.
- **The picture you already have is not rescaled.** Pick it again (or wear a preset that holds it) once to get the smaller one.
- New: **`PJSEKAI_CHOOSER`** (environment of the shell, e.g. `hl.env("PJSEKAI_CHOOSER", "kdialog")` in `hyprland.lua`, or `export PJSEKAI_CHOOSER=kdialog` in `~/.config/uwsm/env` with uwsm; it only reaches a shell started after Hyprland has it): `zenity`, `kdialog` or `yad` is tried first, before the usual order. For the other symptom, the file chooser being slow to appear: nothing in the shell sits in that wait (the click reaches the script at once and the script's own work before opening the chooser is a few cheap commands), the time is the chooser program starting, so the first thing to try is another one. GTK4 `zenity` goes through the desktop portal, and a Pictures folder with a lot of images makes a GTK chooser slow too.
- Files: `shrink-image.sh` (new), `pick-image.sh`, `services/preset-state.sh` (`restore`), `services/preset-images-test/shrink_test.sh` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — presets keep the avatar and banner

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here). Checked: `services/preset-state.sh` run for real against temporary state / data folders (save with both pictures, with one, with none, overwrite, restore, delete; names with `../` or a leading dot refused; a restore asked to write `../../x` or read `../x` puts nothing outside the data folder); `pick-image.sh load` then finds the restored picture, so it survives a restart; the new logic in `DesktopPresetService.qml` run on its own under node (`services/preset-images-test/preset_images_unit.js`; the function text is cut out of the .qml file so it tests the real code); brace balance of the changes. Not checked: anything that needs the shell drawn.

- New: **a preset now holds the avatar and banner** (the pictures you pick by clicking them on the Quick Panel). Save a preset and the two pictures are copied into `~/.local/state/pjsekai/presets/<name>.images/` (`avatar.<ext>`, `banner.<ext>`), next to `<name>.json`. They are copied because `pick-image.sh` deletes the old picture the moment you pick a new one, so a path alone would point at nothing. The folder travels with the presets folder when you copy it elsewhere (the wallpaper paths still don't).
- New: **wearing a preset puts its avatar and banner back**, live, the same way a fresh pick does (copied into `~/.local/share/pjsekai/`, the old one dropped), so they are also what the shell shows after a restart. A picture that is already the one worn is not copied again.
- A preset saved while **no** avatar (or banner) was picked wears "none": the picked picture is forgotten and the default comes back, like right-click on it. This deletes the picture you were wearing; if it isn't in another preset it is gone. A preset saved **before** this change has no pictures in it and leaves yours alone. If a preset's copy has been deleted from `<name>.images/` that picture just stays as it is.
- The tick on a tile now also needs the avatar and banner to match. Presets saved before this keep their tick as before (it ignores the pictures for them).
- Fixed: **`PanelImages.clear()` dropped a second clear that came while the first was still running**, so clearing avatar and banner back to back left one file on disk and it came back at the next start. They are queued now. Right-click on a picture behaves as before.
- Same guard as the wallpaper's: a preset saved before `PanelImages` has looked for the pictures from the last session (first moment after start) is saved without a pictures part rather than as "none".
- Not done: the tile on the PRESETS tab does not show the avatar or banner.
- Files: `PanelImages.qml` (`loaded`, `avatarPath`, `bannerPath`, queued `clear`), `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (`capture`, `write`, `isCurrent`, `wearImages`), `services/preset-state.sh` (`save` copies, new `restore`, `delete` removes the folder), `services/preset-images-test/preset_images_unit.js` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — desktop presets (the PRESETS tab of Edit Desktop)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (no Qt here). Checked: `services/preset-state.sh` run for real (save, list, delete, wallpaper-file check, and it refuses `../x`, `.hidden` and an empty name); the service's own logic run on its own under node (name cleaning, which Settings keys a preset leaves alone, the "is this the one I'm wearing" comparison, list parsing, wallpaper path collection) and `SettingsService.applyPreset` against a fake settings object; bracket balance of every edited QML file. Not checked: anything that needs the shell drawn, so the likeliest thing to want a hands-on tweak is the name field taking the keyboard inside the shelf (see below).

- New: **Edit Desktop has a fourth tab, PRESETS.** The first tile saves the desktop as it is now (click, type a name, Enter; Esc cancels). Every saved preset is a tile showing its wallpaper, its theme's two accent colours and a light / dark mark. **Click a tile to wear it, live**: colours, bar, widgets, wallpaper and Quick Panel buttons change while you watch the card, no restart. Hover a tile for **SAVE OVER** (press twice), **right click twice to delete**. A tick sits on the tile that matches the desktop right now and goes away as soon as you change anything (the tab compares once a second while it is showing, so it is never a stale "last used").
- How it follows pC (end4's shell): his presets are the whole config file, filtered by a blacklist when saving (`BLACKLIST_FILTER` in `scripts/presets.sh`) and deep-merged back when applying. This shell keeps its state in many small pieces, so `DesktopPresetService.capture()` asks each one for its look and `apply()` hands each one its part back through the same setters the Settings Panel and Edit Desktop already use, so every piece also saves itself as usual. The blacklist is `keepExact` / `keepPrefix` / `widgetSkip` at the top of `services/DesktopPresetService.qml`.
- **A preset holds:** the Settings Panel's switches and numbers, the colour group and Light / Dark / Darker, the bar (style, edge, left / centre / right arrangement, per-monitor bars), the clock, user card, weather, to-do and visualizer (on or off, where they sit, their looks), the Quick Panel's buttons with sizes and columns, and the wallpaper (per monitor when that mode is on, and the lockscreen's own if it has one).
- **A preset never touches:** 24-hour time, weather unit and place, calendar stars, the Files key's command, clipboard / web-suggestion switches, update checks, Pomodoro times, the screenshot picker, the wallpaper video / thumbnail engine, app and system colour sync, the Quick Panel's hot edge, the visualizer's audio engine and performance switches, the clock's 24-hour format, the to-do's tasks. Not in a preset at all: the dock's pinned apps, the shell scale per monitor, night light and other system toggles, "Lock all".
- A preset's wallpaper file is checked first: if it has been deleted the wallpaper stays as it is (a notification says so) and everything else still applies. Per-monitor presets only set monitors that are plugged in.
- From a script or keybind: `qs -c pjsekai ipc call presets list | save "name" | apply "name" | remove "name" | toggle | open`. `toggle` opens Edit Desktop straight on the PRESETS tab, like `theme toggle` does for THEME.
- Presets are plain JSON in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/presets/<name>.json`; copy the folder to take them elsewhere (the wallpaper paths inside are plain paths).
- Changed: the four tab keys on the shelf are a little shorter (33 instead of 42) so they all fit.
- Watch for on first run: **(1)** the name field is a `TextInput` inside the shelf window, which holds the keyboard `OnDemand`; if typing doesn't land after clicking SAVE THIS LOOK, that is where to look (Esc / Enter return the keyboard to the shelf through `EditPresetBoard.finished`). **(2)** Wearing a preset with a different bar edge or dock edge rebuilds those windows while Edit Desktop is open (same path as changing them in Settings, not tried inside the mode). **(3)** A preset made with "Wallpaper (matugen)" gets its palette a moment after the wallpaper, like any wallpaper change.
- Files: `services/DesktopPresetService.qml` (new), `services/preset-state.sh` (new), `EditPresetBoard.qml` (new), `EditTray.qml`, `QuickSource.qml`, `services/SettingsService.qml` (`applyPreset`), `services/BarLayoutService.qml` (`applyPayload`), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — wallpaper transitions wear the theme's colours (and matugen's)

## Unreleased — widgets over a live wallpaper, dock gap a bit tighter

## Unreleased — To-Do edit row, Sweep removed, Blocks is a staircase, dock gap to windows

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Checked: the four Blocks shaders compile with `qsb` (same `--qt6` targets as `compile-shaders.sh`) and the `.qsb` files are in `shaders/`; the lock shader runs on the CPU shim with no NaNs and exact ends; the shader maths was drawn to pictures in numpy (p = 0 and p = 1 exactly the old and new picture); `services/lock-transition-test/lock_transition_unit.js` passes (12 cases); brace balance of every edited QML file. The dock gap is untested: it depends on your Hyprland `general.gaps_out`.

- Fixed: **the To-Do's delete (bin) button no longer shows while you edit a task's text.** The whole hover bar (grip, pencil, bin) is hidden while a row is being edited; Enter saves and Esc cancels as before.
- Removed: **the Sweep wallpaper transition** (and its lock version): `shaders/sekai_sweep*`, its entry in Settings -> Desktop -> Wallpaper -> Transition, in Random, and its `laneCount` / `staggerAmount` properties. A saved setting of `sekaiSweep` now plays the ring and shows Ring as picked.
- Changed: **Blocks is now a staircase** (`shaders/sekai_blocks*`, wallpaper and lock). The screen is cut into 3 horizontal rows (steps). A pink, a cyan and a white band cross it sideways (to the left, starting at the bottom), and every row is set back by one band-group width from the row before it, so the bands make a staircase. Flat colours, hard edges, same three colours, same duration, easing and speed slider as Ring and Star. First and last frames are exactly the old and new picture. (The tile-wave version from the previous build is gone.)
- Blocks tuning, all in `services/WallpaperService.qml`: `blocksBlockSize` = height of a step (0.34 = 3 steps, 0.25 = 4, 0.2 = 5); `blocksStagger` = how far each step is set back, in widths of the three-band group (1 = default, 0 = one straight vertical wipe, 2 = long steps); `blocksBandThickness` (0.075); `blocksDirection` (only its signs count: x < 0 goes left, y < 0 starts at the bottom).
- Fixed: **too much space between a pinned dock and the windows above it.** Hyprland adds `general.gaps_out` between a reserved strip and the windows, on top of the 14 px the dock keeps from the screen edge. The dock now trims that off the way the bar's Transparent style does (`Bar.qml` `transparentTrim`) and puts back a gap of `pinnedWindowGap` (default 9 design px: the dock's 14 px edge gap less 5, like the bar). So the gap between the windows and the dock is that number whatever `gaps_out` is, and follows the Hyprland setting live. Make `pinnedWindowGap` in `Dock.qml` bigger for more room. Only applies while the dock is pinned (an unpinned dock reserves nothing).
- Run `shaders/compile-shaders.sh` if you change a shader; the new Blocks `.qsb` files are already built.
- Files: `TodoWidget.qml`, `Dock.qml`, `WallpaperLayer.qml`, `LockRing.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `services/WallpaperService.qml`, `services/LockState.qml`, `shaders/sekai_blocks{,_lock}.{vert,frag,*.qsb}`, `services/lock-transition-test/` (test and shim), `CHANGELOG.md`. Deleted: `shaders/sekai_sweep*`.

## Unreleased — Pulse, a halftone dot-matrix band (one build step)

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and the shader is not compiled** (no Qt shader tools here). Checked: the shader's code run on the CPU through the C++ shim and rendered to pictures at 1920x300 on dark and light backgrounds (kick, mid, quiet, Lite): no NaNs, colours never brighter than their alpha.

- New: **Pulse** (style `pulse`; Settings > Desktop > Visualizer style, `ipc call visualizer style pulse`, or `nextStyle`). A full-width band made of a screentone grid of round dots, mirrored above and below a centre line, with the bass in the middle and the treble toward the edges. Dots up to each column's level are lit, shading from cyan at the centre line to pink at the tips (growing bigger toward the tips); dots above the level are small purple specks, so the grid shows even when quiet. Peak-hold sparkles ride the tips and fall slowly. On a kick, the dots flash, the columns jump, and a ring ripples out from the centre swelling the dots it crosses.
- It uses the same direct kick detection as Scope (the engine's levels with no extra easing; a kick is the bass rising above its recent average), so it hits on the beat. Dot size scales with the band height (always 20 rows). No loops and no history, so it is the cheapest of the three on the GPU.
- Performance Lite (`VisualizerService.perf >= 2`): plain dots only, no kick ripple, no glow, no peak sparkles.
- Changed: `shaders/visualizer/build.sh` builds `tide`, `ribbons`, `scope` and `pulse`. Run `sh shaders/visualizer/build.sh` (needs `qsb`) and restart. Until `pulse.frag.qsb` exists, Pulse draws nothing; Ribbons and Scope are not affected.
- Check: (1) build, restart, pick Pulse with music: columns jump on kicks, a ring runs out from the middle, sparkles sit on the tips; (2) colours in Cover / Sekai / Theme modes; (3) S / M / L / XL and dragging the band in Edit Desktop; (4) 80% / 150% scale; (5) Lite; (6) GPU use next to Ribbons and Scope. If it draws nothing or the shader fails, run `qs -c pjsekai` from a terminal and send me the error.
- If it needs tuning (all in `pulse.frag`): dot rows (`sp = H / 20.0`), column height (`0.46`, `1.15`), how much the treble is lifted (`0.9`), kick punch (`0.35 * bs`), ripple speed and width (`0.95`, `0.004`), glow (`0.55`), sparkle spacing (every third column) and the cyan-to-pink blend (`smoothstep(0.3, 0.85, tt)`). The kick sensitivity and fade are the same numbers as Scope, in `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`.
- Files: `shaders/visualizer/pulse.frag`, `shaders/visualizer/build.sh`, `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`, `services/VisualizerService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `shell.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Scope hits the music harder

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and the shader is not compiled** (the new beat logic was run in node against the engine's demo song, and the shader on the CPU through the C++ shim: no NaNs).

- Cause: Scope was not reacting to the music in time. It ran through the same extra easing as Ribbons on top of the engine's own easing (two slow stages in a row, so every hit arrived late and soft), it waited for the engine's beat pulse (which only fires on a strong bass jump), and it spent most of its movement on waves scrolling at a steady speed.
- Changed: **Scope now reads the engine's levels directly** (no extra easing) and finds its own kick from the low bands: a kick is the bass rising above its recent average, felt on the next frame, then it fades over about a fifth of a second. Ribbons is unchanged and keeps its extra smoothing.
- Changed: **on a kick the trace jumps**: the big slow swells rise the most and the high ripples the least, the afterglow copies fan out wider, the line gets thicker and brighter, and the grid flashes. On the demo song the trace's strength goes from about 0.27 between kicks to about 0.7 on a kick.
- Changed: **the waves scroll slower** (about half the old steady speed) and speed up with the bass and the kick, so the movement you see comes from the music; the speed eases (no jumps). Quiet and mid-level music now moves more (the levels are lifted before they set the wave heights).
- Tune: `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`: how easily a kick registers (`* 1.15 - 0.02`, then `* 4`), how fast it fades (`dt * 7`), scroll speed (`0.5 + 1.2 * weight + 1.8 * root.kick`). `ScopeMath.js`: how hard each wave group jumps (`KICK`), afterglow fan-out (`2.5 * kick`), liveliness (`WEIGHT`, the `0.7` lift, the `2.3` gain).
- Files: `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`, `ScopeMath.js`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Scope, an oscilloscope band (one build step)

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and the shader is not compiled** (no Qt shader tools here). Checked: the shader's code run on the CPU through the C++ shim, fed by the real `ScopeMath.js` run in node, and rendered to pictures at 1920x300 on dark and light backgrounds (loud, quiet, Lite): no NaNs, colours never brighter than their alpha.

- New: **Scope** (style `scope`; Settings > Desktop > Visualizer style, `ipc call visualizer style scope`, or `nextStyle`). A full-width band like Ribbons: one glowing cyan trace with a white core, three fading pink afterglow copies behind it, a faint graticule (grid, centre line with ticks), a soft fill between the trace and the centre line, and a few sparkles and specks. Bass makes the big slow swells, treble the fine ripples, the beat swells the trace and brightens the grid, and the waves travel faster when the music is bassy (same rule as Ribbons). It sits flat on the centre line in silence.
- How it works: `ScopeMath.js` builds the waveform on the CPU each frame from six groups of bands (six sine partials, each with its own travel speed) as four traces of 48 samples, sent as 48 vector properties (`s0_0` to `s3_11`) from `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`; `scope.frag` joins the samples with a smooth curve. That is why it is much cheaper on the GPU than evaluating the sines per pixel. It uses the same extra easing and bass-driven travel as Ribbons (`soft` in the layer).
- Performance Lite (`VisualizerService.perf >= 2`): one trace only, no afterglow, fill, sparkles or specks.
- Changed: `shaders/visualizer/build.sh` builds `tide`, `ribbons` and `scope`. Run `sh shaders/visualizer/build.sh` (needs `qsb`) and restart. Until `scope.frag.qsb` exists, Scope draws nothing. Ribbons stays as its own style.
- Check: (1) build, restart, pick Scope with music: the trace swells on bass and the pink afterglow trails it; (2) colours in Cover / Sekai / Theme modes; (3) S / M / L / XL and dragging the band in Edit Desktop; (4) 80% / 150% scale; (5) Lite; (6) GPU use next to Ribbons. If it draws nothing or the shader fails, run `qs -c pjsekai` from a terminal and send me the error.
- If it needs tuning: `ScopeMath.js` has how lively the wave is (`WEIGHT`, the final `2.3` gain), the wave counts (`FREQ`), how far the afterglow trails (`LAG`) and travel speeds (`SPEED`); `scope.frag` has the trace thickness (`lw * 1.35`), afterglow strength (`fk`), and grid strength (`0.08 + 0.1 * bs`).
- Files: `ScopeMath.js`, `shaders/visualizer/scope.frag`, `shaders/visualizer/build.sh`, `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `services/VisualizerService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `shell.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the notch is one piece with the phone

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). From your screenshot, zoomed in: in the straight-on pixel draw there is a hairline between the notch and the dark glass beside it (the notch looked like a separate tab laid on the screen).

- Fixed: **the notch started exactly where the screen starts** (26 design units in), so the notch's edge and the screen's edge met on one line. Where that line is not on the pixel grid (it never is, at 0.76 scale) each shape covers only part of the pixel there, and the two partial covers do not add up to a whole one: a hairline of the screen's colour shows through. The old tilted draw blurred it away; the crisp straight-on draw shows it. The notch now reaches back 14 units into the dark glass beside it (`notchLead`, same colour), so the two are one shape and there is nothing to meet. Done for the landscape cut-out (Settings / Wallpapers) and the portrait one (Quick Panel / Overview), which had the same line.
- Not changed: the notch's size, shape, position and lens (the visible part and the camera are exactly where they were: only the part hidden inside the glass is new).
- Check: the left edge of the Wallpapers phone once it has landed (no light line between the notch and the frame), and the Overview phone's top notch. If a light line is still there, tell me which edge.
- Files: `PhoneBody.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — entering the Wallpapers is quicker

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Found by stepping your screen recording at 60 fps: the turn itself is only about 0.3 s, but after it lands the screen stays blank white for another ~0.2-0.4 s while the gallery is built (it only started building at the tap), then fades in over 200 ms, and the settle wobble holds the sharp pixel draw back for about half a second more. Those were the slow parts, so those are what changed.

- Changed: **the gallery is built beforehand.** While the Apps screen is up (that is where the Wallpapers tile is) the gallery is built out of sight (`SettingsStage.warmWallpapers`), so on the tap it is already there with its thumbnails loaded, and its spin-in is played again as the turn starts (`dealIfWarm` -> `WallpaperFace.replayDeal`), so you still see it dealt. It is let go again when you leave Apps or close the panel. `wallpapersWarm: false` at the top of `QuickPanel.qml` turns this off.
- Changed: **the turn into the Wallpapers takes 190 ms instead of 260 ms, and its settle wobble is shortened to match** (`wallpapersEnterMs`; the sharp pixel draw takes over about 0.15 s sooner). Leaving is unchanged, and so are the Settings' turns. Set it equal to `flipMs` for the Settings' speed.
- Changed: the gallery's fade-in is 120 ms instead of 200 ms (`arrive` in `SettingsStage.qml`).
- Not changed: the tilt, the straightening, the no-hop fix, the wheel's drag effects, and the entrance from the bar key / IPC (it pops up as before, and builds the gallery as it comes up).
- Check: (1) tap the Wallpapers tile on Apps: no blank white screen after the turn, the wheel spins in as it lands; (2) the first time after Apps is shown: give it a second so the gallery can build before the tap (a tap in the first moments still works, it just builds then); (3) leave Apps (back to Quick) and the panel's idle cost is as before; (4) Settings -> Wallpapers is not warmed (it still builds on the tap, with the 120 ms fade).
- Files: `QuickPanel.qml`, `SettingsStage.qml`, `WallpaperFace.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — no hop when the Wallpapers' phone goes straight to the pixels

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). This one was found in your screen recording: stepping it at 60 fps, the whole phone (notch and all) hops about 22 px left and a few px up in a single frame, the frame the pixel path takes over after the landing, and nothing else moves.

- Fixed: **the direct (pixel) draw put the screen's middle on the tilted pose's middle, but a phone that has straightened from the tilt is somewhere else.** The body turns about its own middle (half its thickness behind the front), so the flat front's middle is 27 x 14 design units from the tilted one (worked out from `PhoneMath`: (665.8, 391.9) tilted vs (692.9, 406.1) flat; about 20 x 10 px at 0.75 fit, which is the hop in the video). `PhoneBody.qml` now has `directCenterX/Y` (default: the resting screen's middle, so the Overview phone is exactly as before) and `frontCenter`; `SettingsStage.qml` points the direct draw at `frontCenter`, and uses the same offset when it puts the screen's top-left on a whole device pixel.
- Not changed: the tilt, the straightening, the turn, the wheel's drag effects. A sub-pixel (under half a device pixel) settle can still happen at the switch: that is the picture going onto the pixel grid, which is the sharpening.
- Check: land on the Wallpapers and watch the notch and the left edge as the ring-out ends (the notch should not move); then Back (it should leave from where it stood).
- Files: `PhoneBody.qml`, `SettingsStage.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- Files changed by the fix: `shell.qml`, `QuickPanel.qml`, `Dock.qml`.

## Unreleased — Weather card, the bar popup on your desktop (new desktop widget)

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here): brackets and parentheses checked, every name checked against the files it uses, and the preset tests (`services/preset-motion-test`, `services/preset-widgets-test`) pass with it. Expect to look at it once and tell me what is off.

- New: **a desktop Weather card** that is the bar's weather popup (`WeatherPopup.qml`) squeezed into the user card's footprint (320 x 276): the same `SekaiPlate` header, the same gauge tiles, the same forecast views, the same `WeatherGlyph` icons and the same wttr.in reading (through `WeatherSource.qml`). It replaces the earlier "postcard" and "Orbit" tries (`DialMath.js` and `services/dial-test` went with Orbit).
- What is on it: the plate (the conditions, the temperature as a white sticker numeral with its unit, today's high and low, and the weather as a tilted dark disc: poke it for a squash and a puff of diamonds); the place on a slanted tab hanging off the plate with two keys beside it, the Hourly / Days switch and refresh (the arrow spins while it loads); the gauges (humidity as a tank that fills, wind as a dial whose arrow turns to where the wind is going, UV as a five-diamond meter); and the forecast, Hourly (a melody: notes on a curve) or Days (a song list: three rows with weather, high and low, a range bar and the chance of rain filling the row). Point at a note or a row and the plate's top line shows its details.
- The view is the popup's own setting (Settings > General > Weather view); the key on the card flips it for both. Feels like, pressure and visibility are only in the popup (no room). Nothing runs on its own: the pop-ins play when a reading lands or the view changes. The place and the unit are the ones in Settings > General > Weather; it polls (every 15 minutes) only while the card is on screen or Edit Desktop is open.
- Same moving, lock, selecting, removing and shelf picture as the other desktop widgets (drag by any part that is not a key), and it follows desktop presets like them (it glides to a preset's spot). The card is white like the other desktop cards: the housing and every text, line and tile colour use the classic palette (`Theme.p*`), so in the first dark mode it is white and in Light and Darker it follows the theme, exactly like the user card. State (position, monitor, shown) is saved in `~/.local/state/pjsekai/weathercard.json`; `ipc call weathercard toggle / show / hide / refresh / resetPosition`. Add it in Edit Desktop (the Weather slot on the shelf) or Settings > Desktop > Widgets.
- Check: (1) it shows up with a real reading (turn Fahrenheit on and off in Settings > General: the numbers follow); (2) the Hourly / Days key flips the view and the popup follows; (3) hover each note and row: the plate's top line changes and returns; (4) poke the disc, press refresh (spins while loading); (5) drag it by an empty part of the card and by the plate, and press each key without moving it; (6) Edit Desktop: the shelf picture, drag, select, remove, add; (7) wear a desktop preset: the card glides to its spot; (8) no network: "Couldn't reach wttr.in" and no gauges; (9) 80% / 150% scale; (10) light and dark modes; (11) a long place name (the tab shortens it).
- Notes: `WeatherSource.qml` still has the `sunriseText` / `sunsetText` the Orbit dial used; nothing reads them now, they are harmless and can stay or go. The card's wiring (shell, Edit Desktop, Settings pages, cheat sheet, service, presets) was already in your tree and is unchanged.
- Files: `WeatherCardWidget.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md` (and `DialMath.js`, `services/dial-test` removed).

## Unreleased — Ribbons travel faster on bass

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and the shader is not compiled** (checked on the CPU through the C++ shim: no NaNs, colours never brighter than their alpha).

- New: **the waves run outward faster when the music is bassy**: about 1x when quiet, up to about 3x to 4x on heavy sustained bass, with an extra push on each beat. It speeds up and slows down smoothly (no jumps), because the shader no longer reads the clock for the waves' travel: `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml` adds up the travel itself (`travel`) each frame at the current speed, and `ribbons.frag` uses that for the waves, strands and twist. Sparkles, speed lines and the halftone still use the plain clock.
- Tune: `2.2 * weight` (how much sustained bass speeds it up) and `1.2 * root.softBass` (the beat push) in `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`.
- Files: `shaders/visualizer/ribbons.frag`, `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Ribbons, smoother and more polished

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and the shader is not compiled** (same as below: checked on the CPU through the C++ shim and rendered to pictures; no NaNs, colours never brighter than their alpha).

- Changed: **the waves are smoother.** Each ribbon's height now comes from a cubic B-spline across the bands (before, a straight blend, which left small kinks and wobble where bands meet) and each ribbon uses a different spread, so they no longer rise together. Loud passages soft-limit instead of flattening at the top.
- Changed: **the motion is smoother in time.** Ribbons gets its own extra easing on top of the engine's levels and beat (a little slower to rise, slower to fall) in `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`; the other styles are untouched. Tune it in the `dt * 11`, `dt * 4`, `dt * 14` and `dt * 6` terms.
- Changed: speed lines fade in and out smoothly instead of popping; the halftone drifts slower (less shimmer); the first ribbon has a soft rim light inside its edge; a tiny dither removes banding in the glows.

## Unreleased — Ribbons, a new visualizer (replaces Confetti; one build step)

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and the shader is not compiled** (no Qt shader tools here). What was checked: the shader's code run on the CPU through a C++ shim (no type errors, no NaNs, colours never brighter than their alpha) and rendered to pictures at 1920x300 on dark and light backgrounds, in quiet / loud / Lite. Nothing was run in Qt's shader compiler.

- New: **Ribbons** (style `ribbons`; Settings > Desktop > Visualizer style, `ipc call visualizer style ribbons`, or `nextStyle`). Neon ribbon waves that pinch to a bright point in the middle and swell toward both edges: a twisting ribbon in the first colour, two big halftone-dotted lobes in the second and third, thin loose strands, a glowing axis line with a star flare at the centre, twinkling four-point sparkles and specks, and manga speed lines (the shell's navy ink) that spread out from the centre and get stronger on a beat. Bass drives the wide swells at the edges, treble the small tight waves near the middle; the beat swells everything and flares the centre. Sensitivity, colours (Cover / Sekai / Theme), size (S / M / L / XL) and dragging the band in Edit Desktop work as for the other bands.
- Removed: **Confetti** (`confetti.frag.qsb` is deleted). A saved `confetti` style loads as `ribbons`.
- It is **one shader and no loops** (`shaders/visualizer/ribbons.frag`), drawn through `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml` from the same levels as the end4 styles. Performance Lite (`VisualizerService.perf >= 2`) drops the halftone, the strands, the speed lines, the small sparkles and the specks, and smooths the swells from one sample instead of several.
- Changed: `shaders/visualizer/build.sh` builds `tide` and `ribbons` (skips a name whose `.frag` is not there). Run `sh shaders/visualizer/build.sh` (needs `qsb` from `qt6-shadertools`) and restart the shell. Until `ribbons.frag.qsb` exists, Ribbons draws nothing; the other styles are not affected.
- Check: (1) build, restart, pick Ribbons with music playing: waves swell at the edges on bass and stay thin in the middle; (2) colours in Cover / Sekai / Theme modes; (3) S / M / L / XL and dragging the band in Edit Desktop; (4) 80% / 150% scale; (5) Visualizer performance Lite; (6) GPU use next to Tide (`PJSEKAI_PERF`). If the picture is upside down or flat colour, run `qs -c pjsekai` from a terminal and send me the shader error.
- If it needs tuning (all in `ribbons.frag`): how far the ribbons reach (`Hh`), how many waves per side (`cA`, `cB`, `cC`), how fast they travel (the `t * ...` terms in `phA`, `phB`, `phC`), halftone dot size (`sp`), speed-line strength (`0.24 + 0.3 * bs`) and count (`n = 84`), sparkle density (the `0.84` and `0.78` hash thresholds).
- Files: `shaders/visualizer/ribbons.frag`, `shaders/visualizer/build.sh`, `ShaderVisualizerLayer.qml`, `VisualizerWidget.qml`, `services/VisualizerService.qml`, `SettingsPageDesktop.qml`, `shell.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — Tide drawn by a shader only (needs one build step)

**Not run on a live Quickshell, and the shader is not compiled** (no Qt shader tools here). What was checked: the shader's crest curve against the old QML path's Bezier in node (same curve, max difference 1e-12 px across the screen) and bracket balance. Nothing was drawn. Expect to look at it once and tell me what is off.

Why it lagged: every frame the QML version rebuilt three SVG path strings, re-tessellated nine Shapes, and redrew a 4x multisampled full-size offscreen layer.

- Changed: **Tide's drawing is now one fragment shader** (`shaders/visualizer/tide.frag`, wrapper `TideShaderLayer.qml`): the three sheets with their navy outline, the hard flat shadow, the foam line under each crest and the nine pins (stem + white diamond with a 2 px border), at the same sizes, colours, alphas and draw order as the QML (back sheet's shadow, fill, outline, foam, then the next sheet, then the pins). Edges are anti-aliased in the shader, so there is no multisampled layer any more.
- Not changed: **the motion**. `TideVisualizer.qml` still runs the same JS each frame (fold, per-sheet smoothing, blur passes, rolling swells, beat lift, pin heights) and hands the shader the finished crest heights (3 x 81 points; 45 in Lite) and the pins. Only the SVG-string building and the Shape drawing are gone, so it moves exactly as before. The fade, the rise into place and the Lite shadow-off all behave as before.
- Removed: **the old QML drawing** (the Shapes, the SVG path strings, the multisampled offscreen layer). Tide is shader-only now.
- **One build step, required**: `sh shaders/visualizer/build.sh` (needs `qsb` from `qt6-shadertools`) writes `shaders/visualizer/tide.frag.qsb`. There is no fallback: until that file exists, the Tide style draws nothing (the other styles are not affected).
- Known small difference: the QML's path ends sit off-screen (28 px past each edge), where the shader does not bother matching them; and the QML's 4x multisampling is replaced by the shader's own 1-pixel edge softening, so edges may be a hair different.
- Check: (1) run `build.sh`, restart the shell, pick Visualizer style > Tide with music playing: same look as before (sheets, outlines, shadow, foam, pins); (2) if the picture is upside down or flat colour, tell me (it is the shader's y direction or a build error: run `qs -c pjsekai` from a terminal and send me the shader error); (3) Visualizer performance Lite (no shadow); (4) 80% / 150% scale; (5) lag compared to before (`PJSEKAI_PERF`).
- Files: `shaders/visualizer/tide.frag`, `shaders/visualizer/build.sh`, `TideShaderLayer.qml`, `TideVisualizer.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Wallpapers drawn sharp on the Settings' phone

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). Brackets balance in the changed files; nothing was drawn. Expect to look at it once and tell me what is still soft.

Why it was soft (found by reading and by measuring your screenshot, not by seeing it run): the density numbers are fine (the screen is drawn at 2x and shrunk exactly 2:1, worked out for fits 0.6 to 1.0). What softens it is the tilt itself: a tilted screen is resampled once into the body's big picture and again down to the monitor, off the pixel grid. The Overview phone had the same problem and the same cure.

- Changed: **the gallery faces straight on once it has landed and is still, and its screen goes straight to the device pixels** (`SettingsStage.qml`: `straightK`, `direct`, `PhoneBody.faceDirect`, same code path as the Overview phone). During the turn in and out, and the entrance's ring-out, it is tilted exactly like the Settings; the tilt eases out as it lands.
- Changed: going Settings -> Wallpapers on the phone straightens it (300 ms) as the gallery fades in; Back to the Settings tilts it again.
- Not changed: **every drag effect on the wheel** (the swing of the gondolas, the spin and its carry-on, the pop when it rests, the hot lift, the dealt spin-in) and the Settings' own tilted pose. `WallpaperWheel.qml` and `WallpaperFace.qml` are untouched.
- If you want the gallery to rest tilted like the Settings (a little softer), set `wallpapersStraight: false` at the top of `QuickPanel.qml`.
- Check: (1) the turn out of the Quick Panel: still the tilted roll, landing straight with no jump when it switches to the pixel path (if something flashes, set `wallpapersStraight: false` and tell me what you saw); (2) text and thumbnails once landed, next to the Quick Panel's; (3) drag the wheel, flick it, click a neighbour: swing and spin as before; (4) Settings -> Wallpapers -> Back: straightens and tilts again without the Settings jumping; (5) 80% / 125% scale.
- Files: `SettingsStage.qml`, `QuickPanel.qml`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Unreleased — the Wallpapers share the Settings' phone

**Not run on a live Quickshell** (none here). What was checked: brackets balance in every changed file and `node services/route-test/route_unit.js` still passes. Nothing was drawn. Expect to look at it once and tell me what is off.

- Changed: the Apps screen's **Wallpapers tile**, `Ctrl+3`, `ipc call phone go wallpapers`, the bar's wallpaper key and `ipc call wallpaperphone toggle|show|hide` all open it on this phone (`QuickPanel.openWallpapers`, `toggleWallpapersOn`). `shell.qml`'s `wallpaperEntry` points at the Quick Panel.
